     1	from __future__ import annotations
     2	
     3	import sympy as sp
     4	from sympy.calculus.euler import euler_equations
     5	from sympy.functions.special.spherical_harmonics import Ynm
     6	
     7	
     8	def assert_zero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
     9	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    10	    if residue != 0:
    11	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} failed: {sp.sstr(residue)}")
    12	
    13	
    14	def assert_nonzero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    15	    value = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    16	    if value == 0:
    17	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} unexpectedly vanished")
    18	
    19	
    20	def boundary_value(expr: sp.Expr, var: sp.Symbol) -> sp.Expr:
    21	    return sp.simplify(sp.limit(expr, var, sp.oo) - sp.limit(expr, var, -sp.oo))
    22	
    23	
    24	def main() -> None:
    25	    # Exact nonlinear Euler-Lagrange derivation in a local orthonormal angular chart.
    26	    t_exact, w_exact, u_exact, v_exact = sp.symbols('t_exact w_exact u_exact v_exact', real=True)
    27	    Rfield = sp.Function('R')(t_exact, w_exact, u_exact, v_exact)
    28	    muSigma = sp.Function('muSigma')(Rfield, w_exact)
    29	    TwSigma = sp.Function('TwSigma')(Rfield, w_exact)
    30	    TOmegaSigma = sp.Function('TOmegaSigma')(Rfield, w_exact)
    31	    USigma = sp.Function('USigma')(Rfield, w_exact)
    32	    Rt_exact = sp.diff(Rfield, t_exact)
    33	    Rw_exact = sp.diff(Rfield, w_exact)
    34	    Ru_exact = sp.diff(Rfield, u_exact)
    35	    Rv_exact = sp.diff(Rfield, v_exact)
    36	    L_exact = (
    37	        sp.Rational(1, 2) * muSigma * Rt_exact**2
    38	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TwSigma * Rw_exact**2
    39	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TOmegaSigma * (Ru_exact**2 + Rv_exact**2)
    40	        - USigma
    41	    )
    42	    exact_el = euler_equations(L_exact, Rfield, [t_exact, w_exact, u_exact, v_exact])[0].lhs
    43	    exact_el_lhs = (
    44	        sp.diff(muSigma * Rt_exact, t_exact)
    45	        - sp.diff(TwSigma * Rw_exact, w_exact)
    46	        - sp.diff(TOmegaSigma * Ru_exact, u_exact)
    47	        - sp.diff(TOmegaSigma * Rv_exact, v_exact)
    48	        - sp.diff(muSigma, Rfield) * Rt_exact**2 / 2
    49	        + sp.diff(TwSigma, Rfield) * Rw_exact**2 / 2
    50	        + sp.diff(TOmegaSigma, Rfield) * (Ru_exact**2 + Rv_exact**2) / 2
    51	        + sp.diff(USigma, Rfield)
    52	    )
    53	    assert_zero('exact nonlinear Euler-Lagrange equation', exact_el + exact_el_lhs)
    54	    mutated_exact_el_lhs = exact_el_lhs - 2 * sp.diff(USigma, Rfield)
    55	    assert_nonzero('mutated nonlinear Euler-Lagrange sign should fail', exact_el + mutated_exact_el_lhs)
    56	
    57	    w_ibp = sp.symbols('w_ibp', real=True)
    58	    Bfun = sp.Function('B')(w_ibp)
    59	    Afun = sp.Function('A')(w_ibp)
    60	    etaf = sp.Function('eta')(w_ibp)
    61	    linear_boundary_density = -Bfun * etaf
    62	    linear_bulk_density = sp.diff(Bfun, w_ibp) * etaf
    63	    quad_boundary_density = -Afun * etaf**2 / 2
    64	    quad_bulk_density = sp.diff(Afun, w_ibp) * etaf**2 / 2
    65	    assert_zero(
    66	        'generic linear IBP identity',
    67	        -Bfun * sp.diff(etaf, w_ibp) - (sp.diff(linear_boundary_density, w_ibp) + linear_bulk_density),
    68	    )
    69	    assert_zero(
    70	        'generic quadratic IBP identity',
    71	        -Afun * etaf * sp.diff(etaf, w_ibp) - (sp.diff(quad_boundary_density, w_ibp) + quad_bulk_density),
    72	    )
    73	    assert_nonzero(
    74	        'mutated quadratic IBP sign should fail',
    75	        -Afun * etaf * sp.diff(etaf, w_ibp) - (sp.diff(-quad_boundary_density, w_ibp) + quad_bulk_density),
    76	    )
    77	    Bfun_concrete = (1 + w_ibp**2) * sp.exp(-w_ibp**2)
    78	    Bfun_lorentz = sp.exp(-w_ibp**2)
    79	    Bfun_lorentz_endpoint = w_ibp * sp.sqrt(1 + w_ibp**2)
    80	    Afun_concrete = (1 + w_ibp**2 / 2) * sp.exp(-w_ibp**2)
    81	    eta_concrete = sp.exp(-w_ibp**2 / 2)
    82	    eta_lorentz = 1 / (1 + w_ibp**2)
    83	    nonzero_boundary_probe = boundary_value(sp.atan(w_ibp), w_ibp)
    84	    assert_nonzero('boundary operator detects nonzero endpoint discharge', nonzero_boundary_probe)
    85	    linear_boundary_concrete = boundary_value(-Bfun_concrete * eta_concrete, w_ibp)
    86	    linear_lorentz_density = sp.together(-Bfun_lorentz * eta_lorentz)
    87	    linear_boundary_lorentz = boundary_value(linear_lorentz_density, w_ibp)
    88	    assert_nonzero(
    89	        'lorentzian linear probe keeps nontrivial denominator',
    90	        sp.together(linear_lorentz_density).as_numer_denom()[1] - 1,
    91	    )
    92	    linear_boundary_lorentz_endpoint = boundary_value(-Bfun_lorentz_endpoint * eta_lorentz, w_ibp)
    93	    linear_cross_concrete = sp.integrate(-Bfun_concrete * sp.diff(eta_concrete, w_ibp), (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo))
    94	    linear_bulk_concrete = sp.integrate(sp.diff(Bfun_concrete, w_ibp) * eta_concrete, (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo))
    95	    quad_boundary_concrete = boundary_value(-Afun_concrete * eta_concrete**2 / 2, w_ibp)
    96	    quad_boundary_lorentz = boundary_value(-Afun_concrete * eta_lorentz**2 / 2, w_ibp)
    97	    quad_cross_concrete = sp.integrate(
    98	        -Afun_concrete * eta_concrete * sp.diff(eta_concrete, w_ibp),
    99	        (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo),
   100	    )
   101	    quad_bulk_concrete = sp.integrate(
   102	        sp.diff(Afun_concrete, w_ibp) * eta_concrete**2 / 2,
   103	        (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo),
   104	    )
   105	    assert_zero('concrete linear IBP boundary discharge', linear_boundary_concrete)
   106	    assert_zero('lorentzian-profile linear IBP boundary discharge', linear_boundary_lorentz)
   107	    assert_zero('lorentzian finite-endpoint boundary discharge', linear_boundary_lorentz_endpoint + 2)
   108	    assert_zero('concrete linear IBP with boundary', linear_cross_concrete - (linear_boundary_concrete + linear_bulk_concrete))
   109	    assert_zero('concrete quadratic IBP boundary discharge', quad_boundary_concrete)
   110	    assert_zero('lorentzian-profile quadratic IBP boundary discharge', quad_boundary_lorentz)
   111	    assert_zero('concrete quadratic IBP with boundary', quad_cross_concrete - (quad_boundary_concrete + quad_bulk_concrete))
   112	
   113	    # Small symbolic model for the minimal gauge-fixed nonlinear throat action
   114	    # expanded around a static isotropic background R0(w).
   115	    eps = sp.symbols('eps', real=True)
   116	    eta = sp.symbols('eta', real=True)
   117	    eta_t, eta_w = sp.symbols('eta_t eta_w', real=True)
   118	    grad2 = sp.symbols('grad2', real=True, nonnegative=True)  # |∇_Ω eta|^2
   119	    R0, R0p = sp.symbols('R0 R0p', real=True)
   120	    mu0, Tw0, TO0, U0 = sp.symbols('mu0 Tw0 TO0 U0', real=True)
   121	    TwR0, TwRR0, UR0, URR0 = sp.symbols('TwR0 TwRR0 UR0 URR0', real=True)
   122	    d_TwR_R0p = sp.symbols('d_TwR_R0p', real=True)  # d/dw [Tw_R(R0,w) R0']
   123	    d_Tw_R0p = sp.symbols('d_Tw_R0p', real=True)    # d/dw [Tw(R0,w) R0']
   124	
   125	    # Local Taylor expansions around the static isotropic background.
   126	    mu = mu0  # Higher derivatives do not contribute at quadratic order because R0_t = 0.
   127	    Tw = Tw0 + eps * TwR0 * eta + sp.Rational(1, 2) * eps**2 * TwRR0 * eta**2
   128	    TO = TO0  # Higher derivatives do not contribute at quadratic order because ∇_Ω R0 = 0.
   129	    U = U0 + eps * UR0 * eta + sp.Rational(1, 2) * eps**2 * URR0 * eta**2
   130	
   131	    Rt = eps * eta_t
   132	    Rw = R0p + eps * eta_w
   133	
   134	    L = sp.Rational(1, 2) * mu * Rt**2 - sp.Rational(1, 2) * Tw * Rw**2 - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TO * (eps**2 * grad2) - U
   135	    Lexp = sp.expand(L)
   136	    L1 = sp.expand(sp.diff(Lexp, eps).subs(eps, 0))
   137	    L2_raw = sp.expand(sp.diff(Lexp, eps, 2).subs(eps, 0) / 2)
   138	    assert_zero(
   139	        'linear density before IBP',
   140	        L1 - (-Tw0 * R0p * eta_w - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TwR0 * R0p**2 * eta - UR0 * eta),
   141	    )
   142	
   143	    # The linear term becomes a background equation plus a total derivative after integrating by parts.
   144	    linear_bulk = sp.expand(d_Tw_R0p - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TwR0 * R0p**2 - UR0)
   145	    linear_after_ibp = sp.expand((L1 + Tw0 * R0p * eta_w) + d_Tw_R0p * eta)
   146	    assert_zero('linear background coefficient after IBP', linear_after_ibp - linear_bulk * eta)
   147	
   148	    # The quadratic cross term -TwR0*R0p*eta*eta_w is integrated by parts.
   149	    A = TwR0 * R0p
   150	    # Replacement after integration by parts:
   151	    cross_after_ibp = sp.Rational(1, 2) * d_TwR_R0p * eta**2
   152	    cross_term = -A * eta * eta_w
   153	    assert_zero('raw quadratic cross term', sp.diff(sp.diff(L2_raw, eta), eta_w) + A)
   154	
   155	    K_eta = sp.expand(URR0 - d_TwR_R0p + sp.Rational(1, 2) * TwRR0 * R0p**2)
   156	    L2_ibp = sp.expand(L2_raw - cross_term + cross_after_ibp)
   157	    canonical_L2 = sp.expand(
   158	        sp.Rational(1, 2) * mu0 * eta_t**2
   159	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * Tw0 * eta_w**2
   160	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TO0 * grad2
   161	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * K_eta * eta**2
   162	    )
   163	    assert_zero('quadratic density after IBP', L2_ibp - canonical_L2)
   164	    canonical_L2_mutated = sp.expand(
   165	        sp.Rational(1, 2) * mu0 * eta_t**2
   166	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * Tw0 * eta_w**2
   167	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * TO0 * grad2
   168	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * (URR0 + d_TwR_R0p + sp.Rational(1, 2) * TwRR0 * R0p**2) * eta**2
   169	    )
   170	    assert_nonzero('mutated K_eta sign should fail', L2_ibp - canonical_L2_mutated)
   171	
   172	    th, ph = sp.symbols('th ph', real=True)
   173	    Y20 = sp.expand_func(Ynm(2, 0, th, ph))
   174	    spherical_laplacian_Y20 = sp.simplify(
   175	        sp.diff(sp.sin(th) * sp.diff(Y20, th), th) / sp.sin(th)
   176	        + sp.diff(Y20, ph, 2) / sp.sin(th)**2
   177	    )
   178	    assert_zero('Y20 spherical-Laplacian eigenvalue', spherical_laplacian_Y20 + 6 * Y20)
   179	    assert_nonzero('mutated Y20 spherical-Laplacian eigenvalue should fail', spherical_laplacian_Y20 + 5 * Y20)
   180	
   181	    t_mode, w_mode = sp.symbols('t_mode w_mode', real=True)
   182	    q = sp.Function('q')(t_mode, w_mode)
   183	    mu_mode = sp.Function('mu_mode')(w_mode)
   184	    Tw_mode = sp.Function('Tw_mode')(w_mode)
   185	    TO_mode = sp.Function('TO_mode')(w_mode)
   186	    K_mode = sp.Function('K_mode')(w_mode)
   187	    q_t = sp.diff(q, t_mode)
   188	    q_w = sp.diff(q, w_mode)
   189	    angular_norm = sp.simplify(sp.integrate(sp.sin(th) * Y20**2, (ph, 0, 2 * sp.pi), (th, 0, sp.pi)))
   190	    angular_grad = sp.simplify(
   191	        sp.integrate(
   192	            sp.sin(th) * (sp.diff(Y20, th)**2 + sp.diff(Y20, ph)**2 / sp.sin(th)**2),
   193	            (ph, 0, 2 * sp.pi),
   194	            (th, 0, sp.pi),
   195	        )
   196	    )
   197	    projected_modal_density = sp.simplify(
   198	        angular_norm * (mu_mode * q_t**2 / 2 - Tw_mode * q_w**2 / 2 - K_mode * q**2 / 2)
   199	        - angular_grad * TO_mode * q**2 / 2
   200	    )

# Batch I.1 v2 audit — paper alignment resolutions

This file holds the 7 questions raised by the v2 (paper-grounded) red-team audit pass of batch I.1 (stages 001–012). Each question describes a script ↔ paper disagreement and asks for a direction.

## How to answer

Each question has an empty `## Recommendation` block. Fill it in by writing:

```yaml
direction: a    # or b, c, skip
rationale: |
  1-3 sentences explaining the choice.
```

If you do not have a high-confidence answer for a question, write `direction: skip` with a one-line note saying why (e.g., "need to check upstream convention in stage X first" or "depends on physical interpretation I'm not sure about"). **Do not guess.** Skipped questions will be reviewed manually.

**Only edit this file.** Do not touch any script, paper, or notes file. Recommendations are applied by a separate workflow after review.

When done, write `summary: X answered, Y skipped` at the end of the file (X+Y = 7).

---

## Q1 — Stage 001 F1: modal-wall PDE source sign

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_001.tex` (~line 161)
- Notes: `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift.md` (~line 361)
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_sympy_audit.py:189,191`
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_mathematica_audit.wl:188,191`
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_001.md`

**The disagreement:**
Paper and notes write `+(S_lm^(psi,A) + f_lm^ext)` on the RHS of the modal-wall PDE. The SymPy and Mathematica scripts encode the source as `-q · source_total` in the Lagrangian, which produces `-(S_lm + f_lm^ext)` on the equation RHS. The script's summary line claims it verifies the paper's positive-RHS form, but the assertion exercises the opposite sign.

**Options:**
- (a) Paper is correct → flip script source-coupling sign at the 4 line numbers above
- (b) Script is correct → flip paper text to `= -(S_lm^(psi,A) + f_lm^ext)`
- (c) Both derived from a third source that contradicts both → audit stages 002+003 to identify upstream convention author

## Recommendation

direction: a
rationale: |
  The paper and source notes agree on the positive RHS source convention in the modal wall PDE (`paper/stages/stage_001.tex:158-162`, `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift.md:357-361`).  With the scripts' current `-q*source_total` coupling, the Euler equation is the opposite-sign source equation, so the scripts should flip the source coupling and target sign.

## Apply: done
files_changed:
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_sympy_audit.py:189-191: flipped the modal source coupling and target to the positive RHS convention.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_mathematica_audit.wl:188-191: flipped the modal source coupling and target to the positive RHS convention.
sanity_check: "sympy + mathematica exit 0"
notes: Applied with Q2; stage 001 was run once after both edits.

---

## Q2 — Stage 001 F2: gauge-fix term sign (metric signature)

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_001.tex` (Section "Localized Maxwell" / gauge-fix term)
- Notes: `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift.md`
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_sympy_audit.py:211,225,226`
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_mathematica_audit.wl:205,210,211`
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_001.md`

**The disagreement:**
The gauge-fix term sign in the linearized Maxwell sector disagrees between script and paper. The disagreement may be a metric-signature convention difference that the paper card never states.

**Options:**
- (a) Mostly-minus signature `(+,-,-,-,-)` → script is correct as-is; add a one-line metric-signature statement to the paper card or script docstring
- (b) Mostly-plus signature `(-,+,+,+,+)` → script's gauge-fix term sign is wrong; flip it at the 6 line numbers above
- (c) Non-standard convention (or `Z(w)` absorbs a non-trivial wall-direction metric factor) → flag for deeper review against the parent-theory definition document

## Recommendation

direction: b
rationale: |
  The project-wide parent convention is mostly-plus, with `eta_{MN}=diag(-1,+1,+1,+1,+1)` stated in `paper/frontmatter/03_notation_firewall.tex:88` and `paper/parts/part01_parent_geometry.tex:132`.  Under that convention the paper's `+(1/xi) partial^N(...)` gauge-fix term has the positive spatial-coordinate sign, while the stage 001 scripts assert the negative sign.

## Apply: done
files_changed:
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_sympy_audit.py:209-226: flipped the representative gauge-fix Lagrangian and Maxwell targets to the mostly-plus sign.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage001_geometry_lift_mathematica_audit.wl:205-211: flipped the representative gauge-fix Lagrangian and Maxwell targets to the mostly-plus sign.
sanity_check: "sympy + mathematica exit 0"
notes: Applied with Q1; stage 001 was run once after both edits.

## Q3 — Stage 006: gauge-driver placeholder in scripts not in paper

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_006.tex`
- Upstream paper: `paper/stages/stage_005.tex` (defines `L_mix`)
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_sympy_audit.py:7-12,57,66-69,135,142-144,150-152,166-167,182,193-195`
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_mathematica_audit.wl:104,117,119,121-122`
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_006.md`

**The disagreement:**
The paper card and the upstream stage 005 name only `L_mix` (transverse leakage) as the open-system inhomogeneity of the projected Maxwell laws. The SymPy and Mathematica scripts additionally carry symbolic `Gauge_μ` / `gauge[nu]` placeholder terms in both Gauss and Ampère equations (advertised in the SymPy docstring and Section 4 summary as "gauge-driver" contributions). These placeholders are never exercised by any assertion — they cancel trivially between LHS and RHS.

**Options:**
- (a) Paper correct — drop the `Gauge_μ` placeholders from the scripts (auditor's recommended direction: never-exercised placeholder, minimum risk)
- (b) Scripts correct — paper card adds a gauge-driver derivation paragraph
- (c) Future hook — drop placeholders now; reintroduce when a concrete gauge-fixing realization exists (deferred (a))

## Recommendation

direction: c
rationale: |
  The symbolic `Gauge_mu` placeholders in stage 006 are not exercised by the assertions and are not part of the stage 005/006 paper claims, which name only the transverse leakage term (`paper/stages/stage_006.tex:38-43`).  The genuine weighted gauge-fixing channel is handled concretely in stage 008 (`paper/stages/stage_008.tex:13-22`), so drop the placeholders here and reintroduce only through a concrete `H(w)` realization.

## Apply: done
files_changed:
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_sympy_audit.py:8-11: removed the gauge-driver claim from the scope summary.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_sympy_audit.py:57-65: removed the symbolic Gauge0..Gauge3 placeholders.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_sympy_audit.py:130-163: rewrote Gauss/Ampere targets and summaries as leakage-only inhomogeneous laws.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage006_projected_maxwell_vector_mathematica_audit.wl:100-124: removed the gauge vector placeholders from the projected inhomogeneous rearrangement.
sanity_check: "sympy + mathematica exit 0"
notes: none

## Q4 — Stage 007: paper exports `mu_eff^proj` AND `1/xi_eff^proj`; script only verifies `mu`

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_007.tex` (equations (1) and (2), Output paragraph)
- Part appendix: `paper/appendices/stage_appendix_part01.tex:36`
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py` (docstring explicitly disclaims gauge-driver channel)
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_mathematica_audit.wl`
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_007.md`

**The disagreement:**
The paper card's equation (2) exports two observer-dependent projected coefficients (`mu_eff^proj = mu0 · I_WS/I_WZ` AND `1/xi_eff^proj = I_WH/(xi · I_WZ)`), and equation (1) defines three projected integrals (`I_WZ`, `I_WH`, `I_WS`). Both scripts verify only the `mu_eff^proj` half. The script docstring openly disclaims the gauge-driver channel; the paper's `\stagefield{Output}` still promises both.

**Options:**
- (a) Paper is correct → extend scripts: add an `H(w)` profile (e.g., `H(w) = exp(-w^2/rho^2)` with new positive parameter `rho`), compute `I_WH = ∫ W H dw` for the smooth Gaussian `W`, matched-observer `W_match = Z/Z_int`, and regulated `W_eps`; assert closed forms in both engines; assert `xi_eff^proj = xi · I_WZ / I_WH` against an analogous reduction-first reference value; drop docstring lines that disclaim gauge-driver
- (b) Script is correct → trim paper card so equation (1) only defines `I_WZ` and `I_WS`, equation (2) only carries the `mu_eff^proj` line, Output paragraph references only the `mu` channel; possibly re-word the part appendix row
- (c) Both are sourced from a deeper note (the original em_projected source notes were never committed); route the gauge-driver channel to a later stage with a forward pointer

## Recommendation

direction: a
rationale: |
  Stage 007 explicitly exports both observer-dependent coefficients, including `1/xi_eff^proj = I_WH/(xi I_WZ)` (`paper/stages/stage_007.tex:16-28`, `paper/stages/stage_007.tex:37-40`).  The gauge-weighted formula is mathematically part of the same zero-mode projection law, and stage 008 already confirms this channel is real rather than decorative, so the stage 007 scripts should verify the missing `I_WH`/`xi_eff` half.

## Apply: done
files_changed:
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py:7-23: replaced the gauge-driver disclaimer with the zero-mode H(w) channel and xi_eff formula.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py:48-53: added H(w)=exp(-w^2/rho^2) and H_int.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py:84-105: added smooth I_WH and xi_eff checks.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py:142-174: added matched-observer I_WH, xi_eff, and reduction-reference checks.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_sympy_audit.py:179-205: added regulated sharp-observer I_WH and xi_eff checks.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_mathematica_audit.wl:8-18: added rho, xi, Hprofile, and Hint.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_mathematica_audit.wl:36-82: added Gaussian H area, smooth I_WH, and smooth xi_eff checks.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_mathematica_audit.wl:119-168: added matched-observer I_WH, xi_eff, and H=Z alignment checks.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage007_projection_reduction_comparison_mathematica_audit.wl:170-243: added regulated I_WH and sharp xi_eff limit checks.
  - paper/appendices/stage_appendix_part01.tex:36: updated the Stage 007 appendix row to include gauge-parameter mismatch.
sanity_check: "sympy + mathematica exit 0"
notes: Used a new positive rho parameter for H(w), while preserving the Stage 008 H=Z specialization as a checked subcase.

## Q5 — Stage 010 F1: `δu_n` vs `δP_n` (distinct objects)

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_010.tex` (`\stagefield{Output}` block and eq:stage010-du2, eq:stage010-du4)
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage010_*_sympy_audit.py:60-67,71-82`
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage010_*_mathematica_audit.wl`
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_010.md`

**The disagreement:**
The paper card asserts three first-variation identities as the stage's `\stagefield{Output}`:
- `δu_2 = (D_0 z_2 - D_2 z_0) / D_0^2`
- `δu_4 = (D_0^2 z_4 - D_0(2 D_2 z_2 + D_4 z_0) + 2 D_2^2 z_0) / D_0^3`
- `δP_0 = (D_0 n_0 + N_0 z_0) / D_0^2`

The scripts verify `δP_0` exactly but do not verify `δu_2` or `δu_4`; instead they verify `δP_2` and `δP_4` (full numerator/denominator ratio variations including `n_n` shifts), which are distinct objects.

**Options:**
- (a) Paper is correct — `δu_2`/`δu_4` are the intended export; add explicit `assert_zero` checks for those identities to both engines (existing `δP_2`/`δP_4` blocks may stay as cross-checks or be removed)
- (b) Script is correct — `δP_2`/`δP_4` (full ratio variations) are the actual stage 010 exports; rewrite paper card `eq:stage010-du2` and `eq:stage010-du4` to display the explicit `δP_2`/`δP_4` closed forms; rename symbol `u_n → P_n`; update downstream paper text that references those eqrefs
- (c) Both `u_n` (pure D-side) and `P_n` (full ratio) are physically distinct exports — paper gets a `δP_n` paragraph, scripts get `δu_n` assertions (both sides grow)

## Recommendation

direction: c
rationale: |
  The `u_n` and `P_n` quantities are distinct and both are legitimate: `u_2,u_4` are pure denominator-inversion coefficients, while the scripts' `P_2,P_4` are full numerator/denominator prefactor variations (`scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage010_projected_maxwell_push_bundle_master_sympy_audit.py:50-83`).  Since the paper declares shifts for both `Z_n` and `N_n` but only displays `delta u_2`, `delta u_4`, and `delta P_0` (`paper/stages/stage_010.tex:17-37`), the paper should add the `delta P_n` exports and the scripts should add the missing `delta u_n` assertions.

## Apply: done
files_changed:
  - paper/stages/stage_010.tex:39-58: added the distinct full-prefactor delta P2 and delta P4 exports with labels eq:stage010-dP2 and eq:stage010-dP4.
  - scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage010_projected_maxwell_push_bundle_master_sympy_audit.py:50-65: added denominator-only u2/u4 definitions and assertions.
  - mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage010_projected_maxwell_push_bundle_master_mathematica_audit.wl:31-57: added denominator-only u2/u4 definitions and assertions.
sanity_check: "sympy + mathematica exit 0"
notes: Applied with Q6; stage 010 was run once after both edits.

## Q6 — Stage 010 F2: 7 script clusters with no paper anchor

**Files to read for context:**
- Paper: `paper/stages/stage_010.tex`
- SymPy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage010_*_sympy_audit.py:2,85-169`
- Mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage010_*_mathematica_audit.wl:65-220`
- Possibly relevant: stages 011 (P_2 bridge), 012 (primitive bridge), 013–014 (mouth-Taylor)
- Full audit report: `redteam/reports/stage_010.md`

**The disagreement:**
The scripts certify seven content clusters that the paper card never mentions and that have no anchoring notes file in the repo:
- K-surface compatibility
- Transported-target compatibility
- Gaunt Y_{20} lane multipliers
- Weak-axisymmetric trace anomaly
- Primitive static Xi
- Two sign-flip mutation guards

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Master-note audit for step_16_parent_throat_action_bundle_master_notes.md."""
     3	from __future__ import annotations

     5	import sympy as sp
     6	
     7	
     8	def assert_zero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
     9	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    10	    if residue != 0:
    11	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} failed: {sp.sstr(residue)}")
    12	
    13	
    14	def assert_nonzero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    15	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    16	    if residue == 0:
    17	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} unexpectedly vanished")
    18	
    19	
    20	def main() -> None:
    21	    KSigma, MSigma = sp.symbols("KSigma MSigma", nonzero=True)
    22	    B0, B2, B4, Z0, Z2, Z4 = sp.symbols("B0 B2 B4 Z0 Z2 Z4", nonzero=True)
    23	    N0, Ptarget = sp.symbols("N0 Ptarget", nonzero=True)
    24	
    25	    D0 = KSigma - B0 - Z0
    26	    D2 = -(MSigma + B2 + Z2)
    27	    D4 = -(B4 + Z4)
    28	    u2 = -D2 / D0
    29	    u4 = (D2**2 - D0 * D4) / D0**2
    30	    assert_zero(
    31	        "one-pole numerator equivalence",
    32	        (u4 - 4*u2**2) - (D0*(B4 + Z4) - 3*(MSigma + B2 + Z2)**2)/D0**2,
    33	    )
    34	
    35	    K_from_one_pole = B0 + Z0 + 3 * (MSigma + B2 + Z2) ** 2 / (B4 + Z4)
    36	    K_from_norm = B0 + Z0 + N0 / Ptarget
    37	    assert_zero("one-pole K solve", (u4 - 4 * u2**2).subs(KSigma, K_from_one_pole))
    38	    assert_zero("normalization K solve", (N0 / D0).subs(KSigma, K_from_norm) - Ptarget)
    39	    assert_nonzero("mutated one-pole K solve should fail", (u4 - 3 * u2**2).subs(KSigma, K_from_one_pole))
    40	    assert_nonzero("mutated normalization K solve should fail", (N0 / D0).subs(KSigma, K_from_norm) - 2 * Ptarget)
    41	    compatibility = N0 / Ptarget - 3 * (MSigma + B2 + Z2) ** 2 / (B4 + Z4)
    42	    N0_from_compat = sp.solve(compatibility, N0)[0]
    43	    N0_from_equality = sp.solve(K_from_norm - K_from_one_pole, N0)[0]
    44	    assert_zero("compatibility equality", N0_from_compat - N0_from_equality)
    45	    assert_nonzero("mutated compatibility equality should fail", N0_from_compat - 2 * N0_from_equality)
    46	
    47	    dKSigma, dMSigma = sp.symbols("dKSigma dMSigma")
    48	    B01, B21, B41 = sp.symbols("B01 B21 B41")
    49	    Z01, Z21, Z41 = sp.symbols("Z01 Z21 Z41")
    50	    N01 = sp.symbols("N01")
    51	
    52	    D01 = dKSigma - B01 - Z01
    53	    D21 = -(dMSigma + B21 + Z21)
    54	    D41 = -(B41 + Z41)
    55	    K1 = D21 + D01 / 9
    56	    H_even = D41 - sp.Rational(2, 3) * D21 - D01 / 27
    57	    Xi1 = N01 / N0 - D01 / D0
    58	    coeff_matrix = sp.Matrix([
    59	        [sp.diff(K1, dKSigma), sp.diff(K1, dMSigma)],
    60	        [sp.diff(H_even, dKSigma), sp.diff(H_even, dMSigma)],
    61	    ])
    62	    assert_zero("even-gate solve determinant", coeff_matrix.det() - sp.Rational(1, 27))
    63	
    64	    sol = sp.solve([sp.Eq(K1, 0), sp.Eq(H_even, 0)], [dKSigma, dMSigma], dict=True)[0]
    65	    expected_dK = B01 + Z01 + 27 * (B41 + Z41)
    66	    expected_dM = -(B21 + Z21) + 3 * (B41 + Z41)
    67	    assert_zero("wall stiffness slope solve", sol[dKSigma] - expected_dK)
    68	    assert_zero("wall inertia slope solve", sol[dMSigma] - expected_dM)
    69	    assert_nonzero("mutated even-gate determinant should fail", coeff_matrix.det() + sp.Rational(1, 27))
    70	
    71	    D01_comp = sp.simplify(D01.subs(sol))
    72	    assert_zero("compensated D01", D01_comp - 27 * (B41 + Z41))
    73	    assert_zero("expected slopes satisfy K1", K1.subs({dKSigma: expected_dK, dMSigma: expected_dM}))
    74	    assert_zero("expected slopes satisfy H_even", H_even.subs({dKSigma: expected_dK, dMSigma: expected_dM}))
    75	    assert_nonzero("mutated expected slopes fail K1", K1.subs({dKSigma: expected_dK + 1, dMSigma: expected_dM}))
    76	    assert_zero(
    77	        "residual Xi1",
    78	        Xi1.subs(sol) - (N01 / N0 - 27 * (B41 + Z41) / (KSigma - B0 - Z0)),
    79	    )
    80	    assert_nonzero(
    81	        "mutated residual Xi1 should fail",
    82	        Xi1.subs(sol) - (N01 / N0 + 27 * (B41 + Z41) / (KSigma - B0 - Z0)),
    83	    )
    84	    Xi1_from_expected = Xi1.subs({dKSigma: expected_dK, dMSigma: expected_dM})
    85	    assert_zero(
    86	        "residual Xi1 from expected slopes",
    87	        Xi1_from_expected - (N01 / N0 - 27 * (B41 + Z41) / (KSigma - B0 - Z0)),
    88	    )
    89	
    90	    w = sp.symbols("w", real=True)
    91	    beta = sp.exp(-w**2 / 2)
    92	    MSigma_example = sp.integrate(beta**2, (w, -sp.oo, sp.oo))
    93	    KSigma_example = sp.integrate(sp.diff(beta, w)**2 + beta**2, (w, -sp.oo, sp.oo))
    94	    assert_zero("example wall inertia integral", MSigma_example - sp.sqrt(sp.pi))
    95	    assert_zero("example wall stiffness integral", KSigma_example - 3*sp.sqrt(sp.pi)/2)
    96

    24	K_\Sigma=B_0+Z_0+
    25	\frac{3(M_\Sigma+B_2+Z_2)^2}{B_4+Z_4}.
    26	\end{equation}
    27	The outgoing normalization fixes
    28	\begin{equation}
    29	\label{eq:stage019-normalization-parent}
    30	K_\Sigma=B_0+Z_0+\frac{N_0}{P_{0,\rm target}},
    31	\qquad
    32	P_{0,\rm target}=\frac{54Gc_s^5}{5a^5c^5\widehat m_0^{\,2}}.
    33	\end{equation}
    34	
    35	\paragraph{Compatibility.}
    36	The parent-complete compatibility identity is
    37	\begin{equation}
    38	\label{eq:stage019-isotropic-compatibility}
    39	\frac{N_0}{P_{0,\rm target}}
    40	=\frac{3(M_\Sigma+B_2+Z_2)^2}{B_4+Z_4}.
    41	\end{equation}
    42	The audit also checks the response-sign criterion selecting the positive
    43	one-pole branch and the constant-prefactor conditions.
    44	
    45	\paragraph{Output.}
    46	Stage~019 exports \eqref{eq:stage019-parent-bundle}--\eqref{eq:stage019-isotropic-compatibility}.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Parent throat action: isotropic full-bundle bridge audit.
     3	
     4	This script verifies the symbolic bridge from the promoted parent wall block
     5	(KSigma, MSigma) to the isotropic grouped-P2 bundle moments and target surface.
     6	"""
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	
     9	import sympy as sp
    10	
    11	
    12	def assert_zero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    13	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    14	    if residue != 0:
    15	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} failed: {sp.sstr(residue)}")
    16	
    17	
    18	def assert_nonzero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    19	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    20	    if residue == 0:
    21	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} unexpectedly vanished")
    22	
    23	
    24	def main() -> None:
    25	    KSigma, MSigma = sp.symbols('KSigma MSigma', nonzero=True)
    26	    B0, B2, B4, Z0, Z2, Z4 = sp.symbols('B0 B2 B4 Z0 Z2 Z4', nonzero=True)
    27	    N0, N2, N4 = sp.symbols('N0 N2 N4', nonzero=True)
    28	    mhat0, G, cs, a, c = sp.symbols('mhat0 G cs a c', nonzero=True)
    29	    eps = sp.symbols('eps', nonzero=True)
    30	
    31	    D0 = KSigma - B0 - Z0
    32	    D2 = -(MSigma + B2 + Z2)
    33	    D4 = -(B4 + Z4)
    34	
    35	    u2 = -D2 / D0
    36	    u4 = (D2**2 - D0 * D4) / D0**2
    37	    one_pole_defect = sp.factor(sp.together(u4 - 4 * u2**2))
    38	    one_pole_numerator = D0 * (B4 + Z4) - 3 * (MSigma + B2 + Z2)**2
    39	    assert_zero('one-pole numerator equivalence', one_pole_defect - one_pole_numerator / D0**2)
    40	
    41	    P0 = N0 / D0
    42	    P2 = sp.factor(sp.together((D0 * N2 - 2 * D2 * N0) / D0**2))
    43	    P4 = sp.factor(
    44	        sp.together((D0**2 * N4 - 2 * D0 * (D2 * N2 + D4 * N0) + 3 * D2**2 * N0) / D0**3)
    45	    )
    46	
    47	    P0_target = sp.Rational(54) * G * cs**5 / (sp.Rational(5) * a**5 * c**5 * mhat0**2)
    48	
    49	    K_from_one_pole = sp.simplify(sp.solve(sp.Eq(one_pole_defect, 0), KSigma)[0])
    50	    K_from_norm = sp.simplify(sp.solve(sp.Eq(P0, P0_target), KSigma)[0])
    51	    assert_zero('K from one-pole closed form', K_from_one_pole - (B0 + Z0 + 3*(MSigma + B2 + Z2)**2/(B4 + Z4)))
    52	    assert_zero('K from normalization closed form', K_from_norm - (B0 + Z0 + N0/P0_target))
    53	    compatibility = sp.factor(
    54	        sp.together((K_from_one_pole - (B0 + Z0)) - (K_from_norm - (B0 + Z0)))
    55	    )
    56	    assert_zero('compatibility equation', compatibility - (3*(MSigma + B2 + Z2)**2/(B4 + Z4) - N0/P0_target))
    57	
    58	    N2_const = sp.simplify(sp.solve(sp.Eq(P2, 0), N2)[0])
    59	    N2_const_closed = 2 * N0 * (B2 + MSigma + Z2) / (B0 - KSigma + Z0)
    60	    N4_const = sp.simplify(sp.solve(sp.Eq(P4.subs({N2: N2_const}), 0), N4)[0])
    61	    N4_const_closed = N0 * (
    62	        2 * B0 * B4
    63	        + 2 * B0 * Z4
    64	        + B2**2
    65	        + 2 * B2 * MSigma
    66	        + 2 * B2 * Z2
    67	        - 2 * B4 * KSigma
    68	        + 2 * B4 * Z0
    69	        - 2 * KSigma * Z4
    70	        + MSigma**2
    71	        + 2 * MSigma * Z2
    72	        + 2 * Z0 * Z4
    73	        + Z2**2
    74	    ) / (B0 - KSigma + Z0) ** 2
    75	    P2_zero_eq = sp.expand(D0**2 * P2)
    76	    P4_zero_eq = sp.expand(D0**3 * P4)
    77	    const_prefactor_matrix = sp.Matrix([
    78	        [sp.diff(P2_zero_eq, N2), sp.diff(P2_zero_eq, N4)],
    79	        [sp.diff(P4_zero_eq, N2), sp.diff(P4_zero_eq, N4)],
    80	    ])
    81	    const_prefactor_solution = sp.solve([sp.Eq(P2_zero_eq, 0), sp.Eq(P4_zero_eq, 0)], [N2, N4], dict=True)[0]
    82	    N4_one_pole = sp.simplify(
    83	        N4_const.subs({KSigma: K_from_one_pole})
    84	    )
    85	    assert_zero('N2 constant-prefactor closed form', N2_const - N2_const_closed)
    86	    assert_zero('N4 constant-prefactor closed form', N4_const - N4_const_closed)
    87	    assert_zero('constant-prefactor solve determinant', const_prefactor_matrix.det() - D0**3)
    88	    assert_zero('constant-prefactor P2 factorization', P2 - (N2 - N2_const_closed) / D0)
    89	    assert_zero('constant-prefactor P4 factorization', P4.subs(N2, N2_const_closed) - (N4 - N4_const_closed) / D0)
    90	    assert_zero('constant-prefactor N2 solve', const_prefactor_solution[N2] - N2_const_closed)
    91	    assert_zero('constant-prefactor N4 solve', const_prefactor_solution[N4] - N4_const_closed)
    92	    assert_nonzero(
    93	        'constant-prefactor P2 factorization detects mutated N2 closure',
    94	        P2 - (N2 - (N2_const_closed + eps)) / D0,
    95	    )
    96	    assert_nonzero(
    97	        'constant-prefactor P4 factorization detects mutated N4 closure',
    98	        P4.subs(N2, N2_const_closed) - (N4 - (N4_const_closed + eps)) / D0,

   100	    N4_md_one_pole = -5 * (MSigma + B2 + Z2)**2 * N0 / (KSigma - B0 - Z0)**2
   101	    assert_zero('N4 one-pole md form', N4_const.subs(KSigma, K_from_one_pole) - N4_md_one_pole.subs(KSigma, K_from_one_pole))
   102	
   103	    M_roots = sp.solve(sp.Eq(one_pole_numerator, 0), MSigma)

   105	    M_root_negative = -sp.sqrt((KSigma - B0 - Z0) * (B4 + Z4) / 3) - (B2 + Z2)

   121	    if len(M_roots) != 2:

    10	It retains the earlier reduced Maxwell/mixed calculation as the one-port

    12	derivation, not the primary EM derivation.
    13	
    14	\paragraph{Mixed gauge invariants.}
    15	Under \(A_0\mapsto A_0-\partial_t\chi\), \(A_a\mapsto A_a+\partial_a\chi\),
    16	and \(A_w\mapsto A_w+\partial_w\chi\), the mixed fields
    17	\begin{equation}
    18	\label{eq:app-stage021-mixed-fields}
    19	E_w=-\partial_tA_w-\partial_wA_0,
    20	\qquad
    21	C_a=\partial_aA_w-\partial_wA_a
    22	\end{equation}
    23	are gauge invariant.
    24	
    25	\paragraph{Reduced lane.}
    26	For one harmonic lane, eliminating the brane-like coordinate \(A_l\) and the
    27	mixed coordinate \(W_l\) gives
    28	\begin{equation}
    29	\label{eq:app-stage021-self-energy}
    30	\Sigma_l^{({\rm EM}+{\rm mix},{\rm cons})}(\omega)
    31	=
    32	\frac{g_{A,l}^2W_l(\omega)+2g_{A,l}g_{W,l}R_l+g_{W,l}^2A_l(\omega)}
    33	{A_l(\omega)W_l(\omega)-R_l^2}.
    34	\end{equation}
    35	The low-frequency conservative coefficients are
    36	\begin{align}
    37	\label{eq:app-stage021-z-coeffs}
    38	z_0&=\frac{N_0^{\rm cons}}{\Delta_0},\\
    39	z_2&=\frac{N_0^{\rm cons}S_2-G_2\Delta_0}{\Delta_0^2},\\
    40	z_4&=\frac{N_0^{\rm cons}(S_2^2-\Delta_0)-S_2G_2\Delta_0}{\Delta_0^3}.
    41	\end{align}
    42	
    43	\paragraph{Outgoing transfer.}
    44	Replacing \(W_l(\omega)\) by \(W_l(\omega)-\Pi_l^{\rm out}(\omega)\) gives
    45	the first-order transfer factor
    46	\begin{equation}
    47	\label{eq:app-stage021-transfer-factor}
    48	N_l(\omega)=
    49	\frac{\bigl[A_l(\omega)g_{W,l}+R_lg_{A,l}\bigr]^2}
    50	{\bigl[A_l(\omega)W_l(\omega)-R_l^2\bigr]^2}.
    51	\end{equation}
    52	In particular,
    53	\begin{equation}
    54	\label{eq:app-stage021-n0-positive}
    55	N_l(0)=
    56	\frac{(\Omega_{A,l}^2g_{W,l}+R_lg_{A,l})^2}
    57	{(\Omega_{A,l}^2\Omega_{W,l}^2-R_l^2)^2}\ge0.
    58	\end{equation}
    59	
    60	\paragraph{Compact outgoing fingerprint.}
    61	For the compact outgoing \(l=2\) branch,
    62	\begin{equation}
    63	\label{eq:app-stage021-outgoing-fingerprint}
    64	\widehat Y_2^{\rm out}(\omega)
    65	=1+\frac{a^2\omega^2}{9c_s^2}
    66	+\frac{4a^4\omega^4}{81c_s^4}
    67	+i\frac{a^5\omega^5}{27c_s^5}
    68	+O(\omega^6),
    69	\end{equation}
    70	so
    71	\begin{equation}
    72	\label{eq:app-stage021-wall-odd}
    73	\delta D_2^{\rm odd}(\omega)
    74	=-iN_2(0)\frac{a^5}{27c_s^5}\omega^5+O(\omega^7).
    75	\end{equation}

    47	def expect_zero(name: str, expr: sp.Expr | sp.Matrix) -> None:
    48	    if isinstance(expr, sp.MatrixBase):

    69	    chi = sp.Function("chi")(t, x, w)
    70	    A0 = sp.Function("A0")(t, x, w)
    71	    Aa = sp.Function("Aa")(t, x, w)
    72	    Aw = sp.Function("Aw")(t, x, w)
    73	
    74	    Ew = -sp.diff(Aw, t) - sp.diff(A0, w)
    75	    Ca = sp.diff(Aw, x) - sp.diff(Aa, w)
    76	
    77	    # With the scalar-potential convention used in the project summaries,
    78	    # A_0 transforms with a minus time derivative while spatial components
    79	    # transform with plus spatial derivatives.
    80	    A0p = A0 - sp.diff(chi, t)
    81	    Aap = Aa + sp.diff(chi, x)
    82	    Awp = Aw + sp.diff(chi, w)
    83	
    84	    Ewp = -sp.diff(Awp, t) - sp.diff(A0p, w)
    85	    Cap = sp.diff(Awp, x) - sp.diff(Aap, w)
    86	
    87	    subbanner("I.1 — Mixed electric and mixed spatial fields")
    88	    expect_zero("E_w gauge variation", sp.simplify(Ewp - Ew))
    89	    expect_zero("C_a=F_aw gauge variation", sp.simplify(Cap - Ca))
    90	
    91	    print("So the mixed fields E_w = F_w0 and C_a = F_aw are honest gauge-invariant observables.")
    92	    print("This is the exact parent-theory reason the A_w / F_{mu w} sector can carry real reduced dynamics.")
    93	
    94	
    95	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    96	# Section II. Conservative wall + brane-like Maxwell + mixed mode reduction
    97	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    98	
    99	def conservative_maxwell_mixed_reduction_audit() -> dict[str, sp.Expr]:
   100	    banner("SECTION II — CONSERVATIVE WALL + BRANE-MAXWELL + MIXED-MODE REDUCTION")
   101	
   102	    t, omega = sp.symbols("t omega", real=True)
   103	    Q = sp.Function("Q")(t)
   104	    A = sp.Function("A")(t)
   105	    W = sp.Function("W")(t)
   106	
   107	    M, K = sp.symbols("M K", positive=True, real=True)
   108	    OA, OW = sp.symbols("Omega_A Omega_W", positive=True, real=True)
   109	    R, gA, gW = sp.symbols("R g_A g_W", real=True)
   110	
   111	    Lred = (
   112	        sp.Rational(1, 2) * M * sp.diff(Q, t) ** 2
   113	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * K * Q ** 2
   114	        + sp.Rational(1, 2) * sp.diff(A, t) ** 2
   115	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * OA**2 * A ** 2
   116	        + sp.Rational(1, 2) * sp.diff(W, t) ** 2
   117	        - sp.Rational(1, 2) * OW**2 * W ** 2
   118	        + R * A * W
   119	        + gA * Q * A
   120	        + gW * Q * W
   121	    )
   122	
   123	    EQ_Q = euler_equations(Lred, Q, [t])[0]
   124	    EQ_A = euler_equations(Lred, A, [t])[0]
   125	    EQ_W = euler_equations(Lred, W, [t])[0]
   126	
   127	    subbanner("II.1 — Euler–Lagrange equations")
   128	    expect_zero("Q equation", EQ_Q.lhs + M * sp.diff(Q, t, 2) + K * Q - gA * A - gW * W)
   129	    expect_zero("A equation", EQ_A.lhs + sp.diff(A, t, 2) + OA**2 * A - R * W - gA * Q)
   130	    expect_zero("W equation", EQ_W.lhs + sp.diff(W, t, 2) + OW**2 * W - R * A - gW * Q)
   131	
   132	    Aker = OA**2 - omega**2
   133	    Wker = OW**2 - omega**2
   134	    Delta = sp.simplify(Aker * Wker - R**2)
   135	
   136	    Sigma_cons = sp.simplify((gA**2 * Wker + 2 * gA * gW * R + gW**2 * Aker) / Delta)
   137	    D_cons = sp.simplify(K - M * omega**2 - Sigma_cons)
   138	
   139	    subbanner("II.2 — Exact conservative self-energy")
   140	    print("Sigma_EM+mix^cons(omega) =")
   141	    sp.pprint(Sigma_cons)
   142	    print("D_cons(omega) = K - M omega^2 - Sigma_EM+mix^cons(omega) =")
   143	    sp.pprint(D_cons)
   144	
   145	    # Direct solve for A and W to verify the Schur-complement form.
   146	    A_sol = sp.simplify((gA * Wker + gW * R) / Delta)
   147	    W_sol = sp.simplify((gW * Aker + gA * R) / Delta)
   148	    expect_zero("A exact solution residual", sp.simplify(Aker * A_sol - R * W_sol - gA))
   149	    expect_zero("W exact solution residual", sp.simplify(Wker * W_sol - R * A_sol - gW))
   150	
   151	    subbanner("II.3 — Low-frequency conservative coefficients")
   152	    D0 = sp.symbols("D0", positive=True, real=True)
   153	    S2 = sp.symbols("S2", positive=True, real=True)
   154	    N0 = sp.symbols("N0", real=True)
   155	    G2 = sp.symbols("G2", real=True)
   156	    toy = (N0 - G2 * omega**2) / (D0 - S2 * omega**2 + omega**4)
   157	    toy_series = sp.expand(sp.series(toy, omega, 0, 5).removeO())
   158	    z0_toy = sp.simplify(toy_series.coeff(omega, 0))
   159	    z2_toy = sp.simplify(toy_series.coeff(omega, 2))
   160	    z4_toy = sp.simplify(toy_series.coeff(omega, 4))
   161	    expect_zero("z0 formula", z0_toy - N0 / D0)
   162	    expect_zero("z2 formula", z2_toy - (N0 * S2 - G2 * D0) / D0**2)
   163	    expect_zero("z4 formula", z4_toy - (N0 * (S2**2 - D0) - S2 * G2 * D0) / D0**3)
   164	
   165	    subs_dict = {
   166	        D0: OA**2 * OW**2 - R**2,
   167	        S2: OA**2 + OW**2,
   168	        N0: gA**2 * OW**2 + 2 * gA * gW * R + gW**2 * OA**2,
   169	        G2: gA**2 + gW**2,
   170	    }
   171	    Sigma_series = sp.expand(sp.series(Sigma_cons, omega, 0, 5).removeO())
   172	    z0 = sp.simplify(Sigma_series.coeff(omega, 0))
   173	    z2 = sp.simplify(Sigma_series.coeff(omega, 2))
   174	    z4 = sp.simplify(Sigma_series.coeff(omega, 4))
   175	    expect_zero("Sigma z0", z0 - (N0 / D0).subs(subs_dict))
   176	    expect_zero("Sigma z2", z2 - ((N0 * S2 - G2 * D0) / D0**2).subs(subs_dict))
   177	    expect_zero("Sigma z4", z4 - ((N0 * (S2**2 - D0) - S2 * G2 * D0) / D0**3).subs(subs_dict))
   178	
   179	    print("z0^(EM+mix) =")
   180	    sp.pprint(z0)
   181	    print("z2^(EM+mix) =")
   182	    sp.pprint(z2)
   183	    print("z4^(EM+mix) =")
   184	    sp.pprint(z4)
   185	
   186	    return {
   187	        "Sigma_cons": Sigma_cons,
   188	        "D_cons": D_cons,
   189	        "Aker": Aker,
   190	        "Wker": Wker,
   191	        "Delta": Delta,
   192	    }
   193	
   194	
   195	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   196	# Section III. Outgoing dressing of the mixed block
   197	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   198	
   199	def outgoing_mixed_dressing_audit() -> dict[str, sp.Expr]:
   200	    banner("SECTION III — OUTGOING DRESSING OF THE MIXED BLOCK")
   201	
   202	    omega = sp.symbols("omega", real=True)
   203	    OA, OW = sp.symbols("Omega_A Omega_W", positive=True, real=True)
   204	    R, gA, gW = sp.symbols("R g_A g_W", real=True)
   205	    Pi = sp.symbols("Pi", real=True)
   206	
   207	    Aker = OA**2 - omega**2
   208	    Wker = OW**2 - omega**2
   209	    Delta = sp.simplify(Aker * Wker - R**2)
   210	
   211	    Sigma_cons = sp.simplify((gA**2 * Wker + 2 * gA * gW * R + gW**2 * Aker) / Delta)
   212	    Sigma_full = sp.simplify((gA**2 * (Wker - Pi) + 2 * gA * gW * R + gW**2 * Aker) / (Aker * (Wker - Pi) - R**2))
   213	    Sigma_first = sp.expand(sp.series(Sigma_full, Pi, 0, 2).removeO())
   214	    N_omega = sp.simplify((Sigma_first - Sigma_cons) / Pi)
   215	
   216	    subbanner("III.1 — First-order outgoing correction")
   217	    print("Sigma_full(omega) =")
   218	    sp.pprint(Sigma_full)
   219	    print("N(omega) from Sigma_full = Sigma_cons + Pi*N + O(Pi^2):")
   220	    sp.pprint(N_omega)
   221	
   222	    expect_zero(
   223	        "N(omega) compact formula",
   224	        N_omega - ((Aker * gW + R * gA) ** 2) / Delta**2,
   225	    )
   226	
   227	    N0 = sp.simplify(N_omega.subs(omega, 0))
   228	    expect_zero(
   229	        "N(0) positive-square form",
   230	        N0 - (OA**2 * gW + R * gA) ** 2 / (OA**2 * OW**2 - R**2) ** 2,
   231	    )
   232	
   233	    print("N(0) =")
   234	    sp.pprint(N0)
   235	    print("So the mixed-sector outgoing port is transferred to the wall with a manifestly nonnegative coefficient.")
   236	
   237	    # Translate to wall-operator convention D = K - M omega^2 - Sigma.
   238	    Gamma_port = sp.symbols("Gamma_port", positive=True, real=True)
   239	    Dcorr = sp.simplify(-I * Gamma_port * omega**5 * N0)
   240	    print("If Pi_out = + i Gamma_port omega^5 + O(omega^7), then")
   241	    print("delta D_wall^(odd) =")
   242	    sp.pprint(Dcorr)
   243	    print("In this wall-operator convention the outgoing branch therefore appears with a negative imaginary sign.")
   244	    print("Equivalently, the normalized response/admittance carries the positive +i omega^5 sign used in the 2.5PN audit.")
   245	
   246	    return {
   247	        "N0": N0,
   248	    }
   249	
   250	
   251	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   252	# Section IV. Compact outgoing l=2 PDE fingerprint
   253	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   254	
   255	def outgoing_l2_fingerprint_audit() -> dict[str, sp.Expr]:
   256	    banner("SECTION IV — COMPACT OUTGOING l=2 FINGERPRINT")
   257	
   258	    k, a, c_s, omega = sp.symbols("k a c_s omega", positive=True, real=True)
   259	    za = sp.symbols("za", positive=True, real=True)
   260	
   261	    j2a = (3 / za**3 - 1 / za) * sp.sin(za) - 3 * sp.cos(za) / za**2
   262	    y2a = -(3 / za**3 - 1 / za) * sp.cos(za) - 3 * sp.sin(za) / za**2
   263	    h2a = sp.simplify(j2a + I * y2a)
   264	    Lambda2 = sp.simplify((k * sp.diff(h2a, za) / h2a).subs(za, k * a))
   265	    Lambda2_series = sp.series(Lambda2, k, 0, 7).removeO()
   266	    Y2 = sp.simplify(sp.series(1 / Lambda2_series, k, 0, 6).removeO())
   267	    Y2_static = sp.simplify(Y2.subs(k, 0))
   268	    Y2_hat = sp.simplify(sp.expand(Y2 / Y2_static))
   269	    Y2_hat_series = sp.series(Y2_hat, k, 0, 6).removeO()
   270	    Y2_hat_omega = sp.expand(Y2_hat_series.subs(k, omega / c_s))
   271	
   272	    subbanner("IV.1 — Normalized outgoing l=2 response")
   273	    print("Lambda2(k) =")
   274	    sp.pprint(Lambda2_series)
   275	    print("Y2_hat(omega) =")
   276	    sp.pprint(Y2_hat_omega)
   277	
   278	    expect_zero(
   279	        "Y2_hat minimal branch",
   280	        Y2_hat_omega
   281	        - (
   282	            1
   283	            + a**2 * omega**2 / (9 * c_s**2)
   284	            + 4 * a**4 * omega**4 / (81 * c_s**4)
   285	            + I * a**5 * omega**5 / (27 * c_s**5)
   286	        ),
   287	    )
   288	
   289	    Gamma5_port = sp.simplify(sp.expand(Y2_hat_omega).coeff(omega, 5) / I)
   290	    expect_zero("Gamma5_port - a^5/(27 c_s^5)", Gamma5_port - a**5 / (27 * c_s**5))
   291	
   292	    print("Gamma5_port =")
   293	    sp.pprint(Gamma5_port)
   294	    print("So the compact outgoing l=2 branch starts at + i omega^5 with coefficient a^5/(27 c_s^5).")
   295	
   296	    return {
   297	        "Gamma5_port": Gamma5_port,
   298	    }
   299

   309	    OA, OW = sp.symbols("Omega_A Omega_W", positive=True, real=True)
   310	    R, eta, gamma1 = sp.symbols("R eta gamma1", positive=True, real=True)

   167

   170	Clear[eta, gamma1];
   171	$Assumptions =
   172	  Element[{omega, oA, oW, r, eta, gamma1}, Reals] && oA > 0 && oW > 0 && eta > 0 && gamma1 > 0;

     1	# Moving-Throat PDE — Stage 021: Reduced Maxwell/Mixed One-Port Normal Form
     2	
     3	## Purpose
     4	
     5	This note is the next reduced-sector derivation after the first conservative BdG–wall coupling stage.
     6	
     7	The previous step did something important but incomplete:
     8	
     9	- it coupled the distributed wall to stable BdG support modes,
    10	- it generated exact conservative self-energies and pole shifts,
    11	- and it gave the first microscopic grouped real `P2` low-frequency formulas on the isotropic branch.
    12	
    13	What it still did **not** do was introduce the sector that can honestly carry a passive/outgoing channel.
    14	That sector is the localized Maxwell + mixed-channel block,
    15	
    16	a) because the parent 4D theory already contains a genuine localized `4+1` Maxwell sector,
    17	
    18	b) because the mixed variables
    19	`A_w`, `F_{\mu w}`, and `J^w`
    20	are suppressed only in the strict far-field brane limit rather than removed from the microscopic ontology,
    21	
    22	and c) because the 2.5PN and 4PN packages already tell us that the surviving universal radiative route is the passive/outgoing quadrupole branch rather than another conservative closure.
    23	
    24	So the right next question is:
    25	
    26	> how do localized Maxwell modes and the mixed `A_w/F_{\mu w}/J^w` sector feed into the wall response, and how does an outgoing `l=2` branch transfer its `i \omega^5` signature to the wall/worldtube operator?
    27	
    28	That is the whole purpose of this stage.
    29	
    30	---
    31	
    32	## 1. Parent-theory anchor
    33	
    34	The exact parent theory already gives a localized Maxwell action and, from it, a bulk field equation of the form
    35	
    36	`∂_M(Z(w) F^{MN}) + (1/ξ) ∂^N(∂·A) = μ0 J^N`,
    37	
    38	with the mixed channels retained microscopically even when the controlled far-field brane Maxwell reduction later suppresses them. The 4D plasma extension then makes the mixed fields explicit:
    39	
    40	`E_w = F_{w0} = -∂_t A_w - ∂_w A_0`,
    41	
    42	`C_a = F_{aw} = ∂_a A_w - ∂_w A_a`,
    43	
    44	and emphasizes that `A_w` is a genuine dynamical degree of freedom whose mixed forces can drive `j^w` and therefore brane leakage. This is exactly the sector we need if the moving-throat PDE is ever going to produce an honest passive/outgoing channel rather than only more conservative support self-energy.
    45	
    46	The earlier 2.5PN audit then narrows the radiative goal further:
    47	
    48	- the universal point-particle route is the orbital/worldtube STF quadrupole branch,
    49	- the compact outgoing `l=2` fingerprint starts at `+ i ω^5`,
    50	- and the remaining gap is the passive/outgoing normalization on the actual moving-throat branch.
    51	
    52	So the localized Maxwell + mixed block is not an optional embellishment. It is the first microscopically available place where that branch can actually live.
    53	
    54	---
    55	
    56	## 2. Exact mixed-field gauge invariants
    57	
    58	The first thing to check is that the mixed fields are real observables, not gauge artifacts.
    59	
    60	Using the scalar-potential convention already implicit in the project summaries,
    61	
    62	`A_0 -> A_0 - ∂_t χ`,
    63	
    64	`A_a -> A_a + ∂_a χ`,
    65	
    66	`A_w -> A_w + ∂_w χ`,
    67	
    68	the mixed fields
    69	
    70	`E_w = -∂_t A_w - ∂_w A_0`,
    71	
    72	`C_a = ∂_a A_w - ∂_w A_a`,
    73	
    74	are exactly gauge invariant.
    75	
    76	That matters because it means the reduced mixed-sector coordinates are not bookkeeping artifacts; they are reduced representatives of honest parent-theory observables.
    77	
    78	---
    79	
    80	## 3. Minimal reduced Maxwell/mixed model in one harmonic lane
    81	
    82	The smallest useful reduced model is one wall amplitude `Q_l`, one brane-like localized Maxwell coordinate `A_l`, and one mixed `A_w/F_{\mu w}/J^w`-active coordinate `W_l`.
    83	
    84	The reduced quadratic Lagrangian is
    85	
    86	`L_l`
    87	`= (1/2) M_l Qdot_l^2 - (1/2) K_l Q_l^2`
    88	`  + (1/2) Adot_l^2 - (1/2) Ω_{A,l}^2 A_l^2`
    89	`  + (1/2) Wdot_l^2 - (1/2) Ω_{W,l}^2 W_l^2`
    90	`  + R_l A_l W_l`
    91	`  + g_{A,l} Q_l A_l + g_{W,l} Q_l W_l`.
    92	
    93	Interpretation:
    94	
    95	- `A_l` is a localized brane-like Maxwell support coordinate,
    96	- `W_l` is the mixed-sector coordinate that is active in the `A_w/F_{\mu w}/J^w` block,
    97	- `R_l` mixes the two internal gauge sectors,
    98	- `g_{A,l}` and `g_{W,l}` are wall couplings generated by the moving-throat geometry.
    99	
   100	This is already enough to show how the wall can inherit both conservative gauge self-energy and, after dressing the mixed block, a passive/outgoing odd term.
   101	
   102	---
   103	
   104	## 4. Exact conservative Maxwell/mixed self-energy
   105	
   106	With the frequency convention `exp(-i ω t)`, define
   107	
   108	`A_l(ω) = Ω_{A,l}^2 - ω^2`,
   109	
   110	`W_l(ω) = Ω_{W,l}^2 - ω^2`,
   111	
   112	`Δ_l(ω) = A_l(ω) W_l(ω) - R_l^2`.
   113	
   114	Eliminating the two internal gauge coordinates gives the exact conservative self-energy
   115	
   116	`Σ_l^(EM+mix,cons)(ω)`
   117	`= [ g_{A,l}^2 W_l(ω) + 2 g_{A,l} g_{W,l} R_l + g_{W,l}^2 A_l(ω) ] / Δ_l(ω)`.
   118	
   119	So the wall operator becomes
   120	
   121	`D_l^(cons)(ω) = K_l - M_l ω^2 - Σ_l^(EM+mix,cons)(ω)`
   122	
   123	before any BdG or outgoing dressing is added.
   124	
   125	This is the Maxwell/mixed analog of the Stage-3 BdG self-energy.
   126	It is purely even/conservative at this stage.
   127	
   128	### 4.1 Low-frequency coefficients
   129	
   130	Write
   131	
   132	`D0_l = Ω_{A,l}^2 Ω_{W,l}^2 - R_l^2`,
   133	
   134	`S2_l = Ω_{A,l}^2 + Ω_{W,l}^2`,
   135	
   136	`N0_l = g_{A,l}^2 Ω_{W,l}^2 + 2 g_{A,l} g_{W,l} R_l + g_{W,l}^2 Ω_{A,l}^2`,
   137	
   138	`G2_l = g_{A,l}^2 + g_{W,l}^2`.
   139	
   140	Then the conservative self-energy expands as
   141	
   142	`Σ_l^(EM+mix,cons)(ω)`
   143	`= z_{l,0} + z_{l,2} ω^2 + z_{l,4} ω^4 + O(ω^6)`
   144	
   145	with
   146	
   147	`z_{l,0} = N0_l / D0_l`,
   148	
   149	`z_{l,2} = (N0_l S2_l - G2_l D0_l) / D0_l^2`,
   150	
   151	`z_{l,4} = [ N0_l (S2_l^2 - D0_l) - S2_l G2_l D0_l ] / D0_l^3`.
   152	
   153	So the localized Maxwell + mixed block already contributes to the conservative low-frequency moments that the grouped real `P2` and geometry lanes are asking the moving-throat PDE to supply.
   154	
   155	---
   156	
   157	## 5. Dressing the mixed block by an outgoing port
   158	
   159	Now comes the new ingredient.
   160	
   161	The mixed coordinate is the natural place to attach the exterior passive/outgoing channel.
   162	So replace
   163	
   164	`W_l(ω) -> W_l(ω) - Π_l^(out)(ω)`.
   165	
   166	The full self-energy becomes
   167	
   168	`Σ_l^(full)(ω)`
   169	`= [ g_{A,l}^2 ( W_l(ω) - Π_l^(out)(ω) ) + 2 g_{A,l} g_{W,l} R_l + g_{W,l}^2 A_l(ω) ]`
   170	`  / [ A_l(ω) ( W_l(ω) - Π_l^(out)(ω) ) - R_l^2 ]`.
   171	
   172	Expanding to first order in the outgoing port gives
   173	
   174	`Σ_l^(full)(ω) = Σ_l^(cons)(ω) + Π_l^(out)(ω) N_l(ω) + O((Π_l^(out))^2)`
   175	
   176	with the exact transfer factor
   177	
   178	`N_l(ω) = [ A_l(ω) g_{W,l} + R_l g_{A,l} ]^2 / [ A_l(ω) W_l(ω) - R_l^2 ]^2`.
   179

   251	
   252	One of the main 2.5PN worries was that a naive scalar outlet could generate a dangerous `i ω` monopole term before the quadrupole branch ever matters.
   253	
   254	The present mixed-sector reduction gives a clean compatibility check.
   255	If the scalar/breathing lane enters the port-active mixed sector only through a **derivative** coupling,
   256	for example
   257	
   258	`g_{W,0}(ω) = η ω`,
   259	
   260	and there is no direct non-derivative wall coupling into the same port-active combination, then
   261	
   262	`N_0(ω) ~ const * ω^2`.
   263	
   264	So even if the scalar outgoing port law itself begins as
   265	
   266	`Π_0^(out)(ω) = i γ_1 ω + ...`,
   267	
   268	the wall-level correction becomes
   269	
   270	`δ D_0^(odd)(ω) ~ i const * ω^3 + ...`.
   271	
   272	So the mixed-sector reduction is **compatible** with the scalar rescue pattern isolated in the 2.5PN audit: the dangerous linear odd scalar term need not reappear at wall level if the scalar outlet is derivative-coupled.
   273	
   274	This is not yet a full scalar no-go theorem, but it is the first explicit reduced model showing how the rescue can happen inside the same Maxwell/mixed-sector language rather than by hand.

    73	```python
    74	    # Paper round-trip: verify Xi matches the paper's closed-form Xi_load = n0/N0 + z0/D0,
    75	    # with the natural identification N0 = P^2/Delta^2 (so that n_0/N_0 = d/ds log(P^2/Delta^2)).
    76	    z0_form = (Delta * q1 - Q * d1) / Delta**2

    33	## What the paper claims
    34	
    35	Stage 013 is titled "Mouth-Taylor master map" and the `\stagefield{Output}` line states: "Stage~013 exports the mouth-Taylor primitive map \eqref{eq:stage007-z0n0}--\eqref{eq:stage007-z4}." That output bundles four explicit identities:
    36	
    37	- `z_0 = ∂_s(Q/Delta)` and `n_0 = ∂_s(P^2/Delta^2)` (eq stage007-z0n0).
    38	- `z_2 = ∂_s((Q*S_2 - H_port*Delta)/Delta^2)` (eq stage007-z2).
    39	- `z_4 = ∂_s((Q*(S_2^2 - Delta) - H_port*S_2*Delta)/Delta^3)` (eq stage007-z4).
    40	
    41	The "Gate feed" paragraph adds two narrative deliverables: (a) the first-order prefactor slope `Xi_load = n_0/N_0 + z_0/D_0` (eq stage013-xi), and (b) the statement that `z_0, z_2, z_4` feed the even gates `K_1, H_even` (whose explicit linear combinations are not given in this card — they appear in stage 014: `K_1 = -z_2 - z_0/9`, `H_even = -z_4 + (2/3) z_2 - z_0/27`). The card closes with "The audit checks the one-sided Taylor derivatives and their dependence on the primitive bottlenecks." The part-appendix row (line 48 of `stage_appendix_part01.tex`) summarises the deliverable as "Mouth-local Taylor map for projected source and flux corrections" with status `\StatusExactClosure{}`. No `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage013_*.md` files exist.
    42	
    43	## What the script claims to verify
    44	
    45	The SymPy script (and its paired Mathematica file) audit five clusters of claims: (M1) the one-sided Taylor projection lemma — integrals of `exp(-u)` and `u*exp(-u)` over `[0, oo)` recover the leading two Taylor coefficients of `X(u) = X_0 + ell*u*X_1 + ...`, with mutated-coefficient assertions confirming the checks are non-trivial; (M2) the closed-form moment identity `∫₀^∞ u² e^{-u} du = 2`; (M3) the chain-rule expansion of `Z(ell) = (Q - H_port*ell^2)/(Delta - S_2*ell^2 + ell^4)` at orders ell^0, ell^2, ell^4 (the SymPy script asserts the literal polynomials; the Mathematica script independently derives them via `Series` of a primitive `Zsource`); (M4) the analogous chain-rule expansion of `N(ell) = (P - G_w*ell^2)^2/(Delta - S_2*ell^2 + ell^4)^2` yielding `n_0, n_2, n_4`; (M5) substantive coefficient probes — `∂Xi/∂P' = 2/P` and `∂(deltaP_2)/∂G_w' = -2P/(D_0*Delta^2)`, plus a nonzero `∂(deltaP_4)/∂G_w'`; (M6) a "mechanism sieve" — that the 2x2 system `K_1|_{Sx=Hx=0} = 0, H_even|_{Sx=Hx=0} = 0` has only the trivial solution `(Q', Delta') = 0` (and the dual for `S_2, H_port`).

    95	## Findings
    96	
    97	### F1 — paper_misalignment
    98	
    99	**Severity:** low
   100	**Subtype:** `paper_missing_script_claim`
   101	**Files:**
   102	- paper: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/paper/stages/stage_013.tex` (whole card)
   103	- sympy: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_sympy_audit.py:106-124, 126-151`
   104	- mathematica: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_mathematica_audit.wl:126-166`
   105	
   106	**What's wrong:**
   107	The script verifies content the paper card does not mention. Concretely:
   108	
   109	1. `deltaP_2` and `deltaP_4` are defined in SymPy lines 106-119 and tested via the substantive identity `∂(deltaP_2)/∂G_w' = -2P/(D_0*Delta^2)` and a nonzero `∂(deltaP_4)/∂G_w'`. The paper card never introduces `deltaP_2` or `deltaP_4`, never quotes the coefficient `-2P/(D_0*Delta^2)`, and the part-appendix line only describes "source and flux corrections" without naming these symbols or their formulas.
   110	2. The "mechanism sieve" (lines 126-151) — that the 2x2 systems on `(K_1, H_even)|_{Sx=Hx=0}` and `(K_1, H_even)|_{Qx=Dx=0}` have only trivial solutions — has no paper-side mention.
   111	3. Both `n_2` and `n_4` are derived by the Mathematica script and asserted by SymPy, but the paper card only states `n_0 = ∂_s(P^2/Delta^2)`; `n_2, n_4` are absent.
   112	
   113	**Why this matters:**
   114	The script is doing more than the paper card sanctions. The paper-side appendix row "Mouth-local Taylor map for projected source and flux corrections" is broad enough to plausibly cover this work, but the stage card itself does not enumerate these deliverables, so a reader of the paper cannot reconstruct what the audit transcript verifies. Two resolutions are possible: (a) expand the paper card to declare `deltaP_2, deltaP_4, n_2, n_4` and the mechanism sieve as deliverables of stage 013, or (b) trim the script to only the four equation-numbered deliverables and move the sieve / `deltaP` machinery to whatever stage actually owns them (likely 014 given the K_1, H_even definitions live there). This is a paper-vs-script scope question, not a math error.
   115	
   116	**Required change:**
   117	See `## Resolve before fix_loop` block in the directive.
   118	
   119	**Verification:**
   120	Once the user picks (a) or (b), a follow-up directive instructs Codex to either edit the paper card (option a) or trim the scripts (option b).
   121	
   122	### F2 — insufficient_verification
   123	
   124	**Severity:** medium
   125	**Files:**
   126	- sympy: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_sympy_audit.py:100, 104, 144`
   127	- mathematica: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_mathematica_audit.wl:113-118, 141`
   128	
   129	**What's wrong:**
   130	The paper card declares `Xi_load = n_0/N_0 + z_0/D_0` (eq stage013-xi) as a primary deliverable. The script defines
   131	
   132	  `Xi = simplify((2*p1/P - 2*d1/Delta + q1/(D0*Delta) - Q*d1/(D0*Delta**2)).subs(subs_der) / mu1)`
   133	
   134	which silently instantiates `n_0/N_0 → 2*p1/P - 2*d1/Delta` and `z_0/D_0 → q1/(D0*Delta) - Q*d1/(D0*Delta^2)`. The substitution for `n_0/N_0` requires `N_0 = P^2/Delta^2`; this identification is never (a) anchored to an upstream stage, (b) stated as a comment, or (c) made explicit anywhere in the paper card. More importantly, the only test on Xi is `assert_zero("dXi/dPprime", sp.diff(Xi, Px) - 2/P)` (SymPy line 104; Mathematica M5a line 141). That assertion probes a single partial derivative of one summand and cannot detect (i) a wrong sign on `z_0/D_0`, (ii) a missing factor, (iii) an incorrect identification of `N_0`, or (iv) an absent `q1/(D0*Delta) - Q*d1/(D0*Delta^2)` block. The closed-form equality `Xi == n_0/N_0 + z_0/D_0` (with explicit `n_0, z_0` from the paper-side definitions) is never asserted.
   135	
   136	**Why this matters:**
   137	`Xi_load` is one of the named outputs in the stage card's "Gate feed" paragraph and feeds downstream into the prefactor slope. A "PASS" transcript here purchases too much: a reader would assume the paper's `Xi_load = n_0/N_0 + z_0/D_0` has been verified, but only a single derivative coefficient `∂Xi/∂P' = 2/P` is actually exercised. If the developer had written `Xi = 2*p1/P - 2*d1/Delta` (dropping the `z_0/D_0` half entirely), the existing assertion would still pass — the `z_0/D_0` part carries no `p1` and so contributes 0 to `∂Xi/∂P'`. The check is anchored to the wrong piece of Xi.
   138	
   139	**Required change:**
   140	In SymPy, add an explicit closed-form assertion. Concretely, before line 104, define
   141	```
   142	z0_form = (Delta*q1 - Q*d1)/Delta**2     # = paper's z_0
   143	n0_form = 2*P*(Delta*p1 - P*d1)/Delta**3  # = paper's n_0
   144	N0_form = P**2/Delta**2                   # explicit instantiation
   145	Xi_paper = (n0_form/N0_form + z0_form/D0).subs(subs_der) / mu1
   146	assert_zero("Xi matches paper closed form", sp.simplify(Xi - Xi_paper))
   147	```
   148	This (a) makes the `N_0 = P^2/Delta^2` identification explicit, (b) tests the full Xi structure not just one partial, and (c) adds zero hardcoded literals — both pieces use paper-side definitions. Mirror in the Mathematica script with a matching `assertZero["Xi matches paper closed form", FullSimplify[Xi - XiPaper]]`. Do **not** delete the existing `dXi/dPprime` check — keep it as the existing substantive coefficient probe.
   149	
   150	**Verification:**
   151	Verifier confirms the new `assert_zero("Xi matches paper closed form", ...)` line appears in both scripts, and that the saved outputs show a corresponding `residual = 0` print line for each.
   152	
   153	### F3 — insufficient_verification
   154	
   155	**Severity:** medium
   156	**Files:**
   157	- sympy: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_sympy_audit.py:101-102, 146-151`
   158	- mathematica: `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_mathematica_audit.wl:119-124, 156-166`
   159	
   160	**What's wrong:**
   161	The script defines
   162	```
   163	K1 = simplify((-(z2 + z0/9)).subs(subs_der) / mu1)         # line 101
   164	H_even = simplify(((-z4 + Rational(2,3)*z2 - z0/27)).subs(subs_der) / mu1)  # line 102
   165	```
   166	with hardcoded coefficients `1/9`, `2/3`, `-1/27`. These coefficients are the load-bearing physics of the gate definitions (they encode the projected weights of the Taylor coefficients onto the even-mode gates), and they match the paper's stage 014 definitions exactly (`K_1 = -z_2 - z_0/9`, `H_even = -z_4 + (2/3) z_2 - z_0/27`). Stage 013 does not state these formulas in its own card; it only narratively says "z_0, z_2, z_4 feed the even gates K_1 and H_even."
   167	
   168	The script's only tests on K_1 / H_even are the sieve checks at lines 146-151 (and Mathematica 156-166): `assert_nonzero` on `qd_matrix.det()` and `sh_matrix.det()`, plus `qd_only == [{Qx:0, Dx:0}]` and `sh_only == [{Sx:0, Hx:0}]`. By inspection, these tests are insensitive to the specific values of the coefficients `1/9, 2/3, -1/27`. For example, with `Sx=Hx=0`:
   169	
   170	  `K_1|_{Sx=Hx=0} = 2*Q*S2*Dx/Delta^3 - Qx/(9*Delta) + Q*Dx/(9*Delta^2)`
   171	
   172	If one replaced `1/9` with `1/8`, the resulting `qd_matrix` would still have a generically nonzero determinant and the linear solve `qd_matrix * (Qx, Dx)^T == 0` would still yield the unique trivial solution `(0, 0)`. The same applies to `2/3` and `-1/27` in `H_even`. Thus the script reports "PASS" for any nonzero coefficient triple, not specifically for `(1/9, 2/3, -1/27)`. The literal values that are the actual physics carry-forward to stage 014 are never actively tested.
   173	
   174	**Why this matters:**
   175	A reader sees that K_1, H_even are referenced and that their derived sieve has the expected trivial-solution structure, and infers that the gate definitions are correct. They aren't — they're correct only because the developer typed in the right numbers. A single-character typo in `K1 = -(z2 + z0/9)` → `K1 = -(z2 + z0/91)` would still PASS every assertion in the current script (the sieve determinant remains a generically nonzero rational, and the linear solve still returns only the trivial pair). The gate coefficients are the load-bearing input to stage 014, so a silent error here would propagate undetected.
   176	
   177	**Required change:**
   178	Add coefficient-specific assertions in both engines that fail if `1/9`, `2/3`, or `-1/27` is altered. The minimal-scope fix is to substitute concrete values for the source-side primitives and check that K_1, H_even reduce to the expected numbers. Specifically, in SymPy after line 102, add
   179	```
   180	# Coefficient sanity: with q1=1, d1=0, s1=0, h1=0 (only Q' nonzero),
   181	# K_1 should reduce to -z_0/9 = -1/(9*Delta), H_even to -z_0/27 = -1/(27*Delta).
   182	K1_only_q = K1.subs(subs_der).subs({Qx:1, Dx:0, Sx:0, Hx:0, Px:0, Gx:0}) * mu1
   183	H_only_q = H_even.subs(subs_der).subs({Qx:1, Dx:0, Sx:0, Hx:0, Px:0, Gx:0}) * mu1
   184	assert_zero("K1 = -z0/9 when only Q' nonzero", K1_only_q - (-1)/(9*Delta))
   185	assert_zero("H_even = -z0/27 when only Q' nonzero", H_only_q - (-1)/(27*Delta))
   186	
   187	# Coefficient sanity: with s1=1 only, K_1 should reduce to -∂z2/∂s1 weight = -Q/Delta^2
   188	# and H_even should reduce to (2/3)·(-Q/Delta^2) + 0 = -2*Q/(3*Delta^2).
   189	K1_only_s = K1.subs(subs_der).subs({Qx:0, Dx:0, Sx:1, Hx:0, Px:0, Gx:0}) * mu1
   190	H_only_s = H_even.subs(subs_der).subs({Qx:0, Dx:0, Sx:1, Hx:0, Px:0, Gx:0}) * mu1
   191	assert_zero("K1 = -Q/Delta^2 when only S2' nonzero", K1_only_s - (-Q/Delta**2))
   192	assert_zero("H_even = -2*Q/(3*Delta^2) when only S2' nonzero", H_only_s - (-Q*sp.Rational(2,3)/Delta**2 - (-2*Q*S2*1/Delta**3 + 0) ))
   193	```
   194	
   195	Actually the last assertion is fragile because `-z_4 + (2/3) z_2 - z_0/27` with `s1=1, q1=d1=h1=0` gives:
   196	
   197	  z_0 = 0; z_2 = (Delta·Q·1)/Delta^3 = Q/Delta^2; z_4 = (-Delta^2·Hport·1 + 2·Delta·Q·S2·1)/Delta^4 = -Hport/Delta^2 + 2·Q·S2/Delta^3.
   198	
   199	So `H_even = -z_4 + (2/3) z_2 - z_0/27 = Hport/Delta^2 - 2·Q·S2/Delta^3 + (2/3)·Q/Delta^2 - 0`. Hence the assertion should be:
   200	```
   201	assert_zero(
   202	    "H_even when only S2' nonzero",
   203	    H_only_s - (Hport/Delta**2 - 2*Q*S2/Delta**3 + sp.Rational(2,3)*Q/Delta**2)
   204	)
   205	```
   206	The Codex directive includes the trap-aware form. Mirror these in the Mathematica script with `assertZero["K1 = -1/(9 Delta) when only Qx nonzero", K1 /. ... - (-1/(9 Delta))]` etc.
   207	
   208	**Verification:**
   209	Verifier confirms the four new assertions appear (two for K_1, two for H_even, in each engine) and that perturbing `1/9 → 1/8` in K_1 would now make `K1 = -z0/9 when only Q' nonzero` fail. (The verifier doesn't perturb; this is a paper round-trip check the auditor did mentally.)
   210	
   211	## Independent-derivation check (Mathematica)
   212	
   213	The Mathematica script is **not** a transliteration of the SymPy script — for the load-bearing M3 and M4 claims (the `z_n, n_n` chain-rule coefficients), the Mathematica script independently derives the coefficients by Taylor expansion of a primitive:
   214	
   215	  SymPy lines 58-69: `z0 = (Delta*q1 - Q*d1)/Delta**2` (literal polynomial).
   216	  Mathematica lines 66-70: `Zsource[x_] := (qFun[t] - hFun[t] x^2)/(dFun[t] - sFun[t] x^2 + x^4); Zexpansion = Normal[Series[Zsource[ell], {ell, 0, 4}]]; z0 = timeDerivativeAtZero[Coefficient[Zexpansion, ell, 0]];`
   217	
   218	The Mathematica script then compares its independently derived `z0, z2, z4, n0, n2, n4` against the literal `Expected` forms (which match the SymPy literals). This is the textbook second-engine cross-check pattern. For the M1, M2 lemmas the two engines do compute the same integrals through structurally similar Mathematica/Sympy code, but that is unavoidable for closed-form moments and is not a transliteration concern. For M5 and M6 both engines apply the same `subsDer` substitution to the same `(K1, H_even, Xi, deltaP2, deltaP4)` formulas — this is parallel use of the same algebraic definitions, not a derivation, so no transliteration finding here either; the substantive findings are F2 and F3.
   219	
   220	## Engine cross-check
   221	
   222	Both engines run to PASS. Final-line residuals match:
   223	
   224	- SymPy: "STATUS: PASS" (no per-assertion residuals in the saved transcript, but the script's `assert_zero` would raise on any nonzero residual; the bare "PASS" implies all 14 assertions returned 0/nonzero as expected).
   225	- Mathematica: 19 `OK ... residual = 0` lines for the `assertZero` checks; 3 `OK ... residual = <expr>` lines for the `assertNonzero` checks where the expr is the (nonzero) symbolic residual. The nonzero residuals (sieve determinants and the `deltaP4` Gprime dependence) are well-formed rational expressions in the expected symbols, no surprise terms.
   226	
   227	No engine disagreement.
   228	
   229	## Verdict justification
   230	
   231	The four equation-numbered paper deliverables (z_0, z_2, z_4, n_0) are now well-anchored: SymPy asserts literal polynomials, Mathematica independently derives them via series expansion of a primitive, and the two engines agree.     34	
    35	The paper card (`stage_014.tex`) describes a "mouth-Taylor gate bridge" whose deliverables are three identities. First, the even-gate definitions `K_1 = -z_2 - z_0/9` (eq:stage014-k1) and `H_{\rm even} = -z_4 + (2/3) z_2 - z_0/27` (eq:stage014-heven). The card's `Output` paragraph then states verbatim: "Stage~014 exports the mechanism sieve: projected EM mouth data can tune the even gates only away from the degeneracy \eqref{eq:stage007-compensation-denominator}."     37	## What the script claims to verify
    38

(1) Structural linearity of the Taylor lift: every bundle slip (`z2d, z4d, n2d, n4d`) is linear in each primitive slip (sympy lines 102-104). (2) Bundle pull-back consistency: directly-substituted `Xi_load`, `K_1`, `H_{\rm even}` equal the symbolically lifted bundle forms (sympy lines 121, 123, 125; mathematica lines 113-115). (3) Sector independence: `Xi_load` is independent of `Sx, Hx, Gx`, and `K_1, H_{\rm even}` are independent of `Px, Gx` (sympy lines 112-116; mathematica lines 128-136). (4) The mechanism sieve: solving `K_1 = H_{\rm even} = 0` over the source-only `(Q', \Delta')` or spectral-only `(S_2', H_{\rm port}')` sectors yields only the trivial solution (sympy lines 135-138, mathematica M5/M6), and the mixed-sector compensation surface has denominator proportional to `\Delta H_{\rm port} - Q S_2`, equivalently `-\Delta^2 \cdot Z_2` (sympy lines 131-132, 144-146; mathematica M9). (5) Constant-prefactor transport ledger: derivative coefficients of `\delta P_2`, `\delta P_4` with respect to `G_W'` (sympy lines 127-128; mathematica M3, M4). (6) Sign-flip mutation test on the compensation denominator (sympy line 133; mathematica M10).

By `sp.solve`'s contract, substituting the returned solution back into the original system yields zero by construction; the assertions cannot fail no matter what `K1` and `H_even` are. The author has self-flagged this on lines 139-141 ("Note: the next two assertions are tautological by sp.solve's contract... kept here for visual symmetry with the explicit denominator factorization assertions above").

Keeping tautologies "for visual symmetry" is exactly the trap the audit framework is supposed to surface.
