   159	## Independent-derivation check (Mathematica)
   160	
   161	The Mathematica script is **NOT** a transliteration of the SymPy script. Its derivation strategy is genuinely independent:
   162	
   163	- SymPy (lines 48-54) defines `z0, z2, z4, n0, n2, n4` as already-product-rule-expanded closed-form expressions:
   164	  ```python
   165	  z2 = (-Delta**2*h1 + Delta*(Hport*d1 + Q*s1 + S2*q1) - 2*Q*S2*d1)/Delta**3
   166	  ```
   167	- Mathematica (lines 52-58) defines the *primitive* `Z0, Z2, Z4, N0, N2, N4` as functions of their underlying source/spectral arguments, then obtains the Taylor lift via symbolic differentiation `D[Z2[Q + mu1*Qx*ell, ...], ell] /. ell -> 0`:
   168	  ```mathematica
   169	  Z2[Q_, S2_, Hport_, Delta_] := (Q*S2 - Hport*Delta)/Delta^2;
   170	  z2d = reduce[(D[Z2[Q + mu1*Qx*ell, S2 + mu1*Sx*ell, Hport + mu1*Hx*ell, Delta + mu1*Deltax*ell], ell] /. ell -> 0)/mu1];
   171	  ```
   172	
   173	These are mathematically equivalent (both yield the linearized z2 in the primitive slips), but they take *different* algebraic routes (closed-form pre-expanded product rule vs. symbolic chain rule applied to an unfactored primitive). A line-by-line transliteration would have copied `(-Delta**2*h1 + Delta*(Hport*d1 + Q*s1 + S2*q1) - 2*Q*S2*d1)/Delta**3` directly into Mathematica syntax; this one does not.
   174	
   175	The same pattern holds for the bottlenecks: sympy defines `K1, He` by substituting into the closed-form z2/z4 (sympy lines 68-69), while Mathematica builds K1, He from the symbolically-lifted `z2d, z4d` (mathematica lines 114-115). The independent-derivation requirement is satisfied.

     7	\paragraph{Source and audit.}
     8	This stage corresponds to
     9	\StageFile{scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage010_projected_maxwell_push_bundle_master_sympy_audit.py}.
    10	It is the master transport from projected EM corrections into the grouped
    11	bundle slots.
    12	
    13	\paragraph{Bundle shifts.}
    14	The conservative and outgoing-transfer moments shift as
    15	\begin{equation}
    16	\label{eq:stage010-projected-shifts}
    17	Z_n\mapsto Z_n+\varepsilon z_n,\qquad
    18	N_n\mapsto N_n+\varepsilon n_n,\qquad n\in\{0,2,4\}.
    19	\end{equation}
    20	For
    21	\[
    22	D_0=K-B_0-Z_0,\qquad D_2=-(M+B_2+Z_2),\qquad D_4=-(B_4+Z_4),
    23	\]
    24	the first response variations are
    25	\begin{equation}
    26	\label{eq:stage010-du2}
    27	\delta u_2=\frac{D_0z_2-D_2z_0}{D_0^2},
    28	\end{equation}
    29	\begin{equation}
    30	\label{eq:stage010-du4}
    31	\delta u_4=
    32	\frac{D_0^2z_4-D_0(2D_2z_2+D_4z_0)+2D_2^2z_0}{D_0^3},
    33	\end{equation}
    34	and
    35	\begin{equation}
    36	\label{eq:stage010-dp0}
    37	\delta P_0=\frac{D_0n_0+N_0z_0}{D_0^2}.
    38	\end{equation}
    39	The denominator-only quantities \(u_2,u_4\) and the full prefactor quantities
    40	\(P_n\) are distinct.  The full first variations in the \(P_2\) and \(P_4\)
    41	slots are
    42	\begin{equation}
    43	\label{eq:stage010-dP2}
    44	\delta P_2=
    45	\frac{n_2}{D_0}
    46	+\frac{N_2z_0+2N_0z_2-2D_2n_0}{D_0^2}
    47	-\frac{4D_2N_0z_0}{D_0^3},
    48	\end{equation}
    49	\begin{equation}
    50	\label{eq:stage010-dP4}
    51	\begin{aligned}
    52	\delta P_4={}&
    53	\frac{n_4}{D_0}
    54	+\frac{N_4z_0+2N_2z_2-2D_2n_2+2N_0z_4-2D_4n_0}{D_0^2}\\
    55	&+\frac{3D_2^2n_0-4(D_2N_2+D_4N_0)z_0-6D_2N_0z_2}{D_0^3}
    56	+\frac{9D_2^2N_0z_0}{D_0^4}.
    57	\end{aligned}
    58	\end{equation}
    59	
    60	\paragraph{Compatibility transport.}
    61	With \(S=M+B_2+Z_2\) and \(T=B_4+Z_4\), the one-pole and fixed-target
    62	normalization \(K\)-surfaces under the slot shift are
    63	\begin{equation}
    64	\label{eq:stage010-k-surfaces}
    65	\begin{aligned}
    66	K_{\rm pole}(\varepsilon)
    67	&=B_0+Z_0+\varepsilon z_0
    68	+\frac{3(S+\varepsilon z_2)^2}{T+\varepsilon z_4},\\
    69	K_{\rm norm}(\varepsilon)
    70	&=B_0+Z_0+\varepsilon z_0
    71	+\frac{N_0+\varepsilon n_0}{P_{0,\rm target}}.
    72	\end{aligned}
    73	\end{equation}
    74	Eliminating \(K\) gives the fixed-target compatibility variation
    75	\begin{equation}
    76	\label{eq:stage010-compat-fixed}
    77	\delta\mathcal C_{\rm fixed}
    78	=\frac{n_0}{P_{0,\rm target}}
    79	-\frac{6Sz_2}{T}
    80	+\frac{3S^2z_4}{T^2},
    81	\qquad
    82	\partial_{z_0}\delta\mathcal C_{\rm fixed}=0.
    83	\end{equation}
    84	For a transported target
    85	\(P_{0,\rm target}(\varepsilon)=(N_0+\varepsilon n_0)/D_{0,\rm target}\),
    86	\begin{equation}
    87	\label{eq:stage010-compat-transport}
    88	\mathcal C_{\rm tr}(\varepsilon)
    89	=D_{0,\rm target}
    90	-\frac{3(S+\varepsilon z_2)^2}{T+\varepsilon z_4},
    91	\qquad
    92	\delta\mathcal C_{\rm tr}
    93	=-\frac{6Sz_2}{T}+\frac{3S^2z_4}{T^2}.
    94	\end{equation}
    95	
    96	\paragraph{Grouped weak-axisymmetric lane.}
    97	The real \(Y_{20}\) square-overlap lane multipliers are
    98	\begin{equation}
    99	\label{eq:stage010-y20-lambdas}
   100	\lambda_{20}=1,\qquad
   101	\lambda_{21}=\frac12,\qquad
   102	\lambda_{22}=-1.
   103	\end{equation}
   104	Thus a weak-axisymmetric lane packet
   105	\(x_A=x^{(0)}+\varepsilon\lambda_Ax^{(1)}\) obeys
   106	\begin{equation}
   107	\label{eq:stage010-weak-axisym-trace}
   108	\bar x=x^{(0)},\qquad
   109	a_x=\frac{\varepsilon x^{(1)}}4,\qquad
   110	b_x=\frac{3\varepsilon x^{(1)}}4,\qquad
   111	b_x=3a_x.
   112	\end{equation}
   113	
   114	\paragraph{Primitive static prefactor.}
   115	For primitive mouth data \(Q,\Delta,P\) with perturbation slopes
   116	\(q_1,d_1,p_1\),
   117	\begin{equation}
   118	\label{eq:stage010-primitive-xi}
   119	z_0^{\rm prim}=\frac{\Delta q_1-Qd_1}{\Delta^2},
   120	\qquad
   121	n_0^{\rm prim}=\frac{2P(\Delta p_1-Pd_1)}{\Delta^3},
   122	\qquad
   123	\Xi_{\rm static}
   124	=\frac{2p_1}{P}-\frac{2d_1}{\Delta}
   125	+\frac{\Delta q_1-Qd_1}{D_0\Delta^2}.
   126	\end{equation}
   127	The sign-flip mutation guards are the nonzero residuals
   128	\begin{equation}
   129	\label{eq:stage010-mutation-guards}
   130	\mathcal R_{\rm fixed}
   131	=\mathcal R_{\rm tr}
   132	=\frac{6S^2z_4}{T^2}\not\equiv0,
   133	\end{equation}
   134	obtained by flipping the sign of the \(3S^2z_4/T^2\) term in
   135	\eqref{eq:stage010-compat-fixed} or \eqref{eq:stage010-compat-transport}.
   136	
   137	\paragraph{Output.}
   138	Stage~010 exports the transport map
   139	\eqref{eq:stage010-projected-shifts}--\eqref{eq:stage010-dP4}, the
   140	compatibility transports \eqref{eq:stage010-k-surfaces}--\eqref{eq:stage010-compat-transport},
   141	the weak-axisymmetric lane signature
   142	\eqref{eq:stage010-y20-lambdas}--\eqref{eq:stage010-weak-axisym-trace},
   143	the primitive static prefactor anchor \eqref{eq:stage010-primitive-xi}, and
   144	the sign-flip guards \eqref{eq:stage010-mutation-guards}.

    11	surface.

    13	\paragraph{Compatibility surface.}
    14	Let
    15	\begin{equation}
    16	\label{eq:stage011-compat-base}
    17	S=M+B_2+Z_2,\qquad T=B_4+Z_4 .
    18	\end{equation}
    19	After eliminating the static wall stiffness, the isotropic compatibility
    20	surface is
    21	\begin{equation}
    22	\label{eq:stage011-compat}
    23	\mathcal C=\frac{N_0}{P_{0,\rm target}}-\frac{3S^2}{T}.
    24	\end{equation}
    25	The projected variation is
    26	\begin{equation}
    27	\label{eq:stage011-dcompat}
    28	\delta\mathcal C

    30	-\frac{6Sz_2}{T}
    31	+\frac{3S^2z_4}{T^2}.
    32	\end{equation}
    33	The static conservative shift \(z_0\) cancels from
    34	\eqref{eq:stage011-dcompat}; it changes \(P_0\), but not the eliminated
    35	compatibility surface.
    36

    38	Stage~011 exports the \(z_0\)-cancellation and the projected \(P_2\)

    14	The primitive reduced packet is built from the denominator

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage012_projected_maxwell_primitive_bridge_sympy_audit.py | sed -n '1,330p'" in /var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""
     3	Projected Maxwell primitive bridge
     4	
     5	This script starts one step closer to the Stage-4 / Stage-5 one-port moving-throat
     6	bundle. Instead of inserting abstract bundle corrections z_n and n_n directly,
     7	it perturbs the primitive Maxwell/mixed one-port data:
     8	
     9	    Q, S2, H, Delta, P, Gw
    10	
    11	by small mouth-local projected-Maxwell slippages:
    12	
    13	    q1, s1, h1, d1, p1, g1
    14	
    15	and derives the induced bundle corrections
    16	
    17	    z0, z2, z4, n0, n2, n4
    18	
    19	exactly at first order.
    20	
    21	These are the quantities that then feed the full-bundle formulas for
    22	D0, D2, D4, P0, P2, P4, Xi1, and the isotropic compatibility surface.
    23	"""
    24	
    25	from __future__ import annotations
    26	
    27	import sympy as sp
    28	
    29	
    30	def banner(title: str) -> str:
    31	    return "\n" + "=" * 96 + f"\n{title}\n" + "=" * 96 + "\n"
    32	
    33	
    34	def line(label: str, expr: sp.Expr | str) -> str:
    35	    return f"{label}\n  {expr}\n"
    36	
    37	
    38	def assert_zero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    39	    residue = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    40	    if residue != 0:
    41	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} failed: {sp.sstr(residue)}")
    42	
    43	
    44	def assert_nonzero(label: str, expr: sp.Expr) -> None:
    45	    value = sp.factor(sp.together(sp.simplify(expr)))
    46	    if value == 0:
    47	        raise AssertionError(f"{label} unexpectedly vanished")
    48	
    49	
    50	def main() -> None:
    51	    Q, S2, H, Delta, P, Gw = sp.symbols("Q S2 H Delta P Gw", nonzero=True)
    52	    q1, s1, h1, d1, p1, g1 = sp.symbols("q1 s1 h1 d1 p1 g1")
    53	    ell = sp.symbols("ell")
    54	
    55	    # Stage-4 / Stage-5 primitive one-port formulas
    56	    Z0 = Q / Delta
    57	    Z2 = (Q * S2 - H * Delta) / Delta**2
    58	    Z4 = (Q * (S2**2 - Delta) - S2 * H * Delta) / Delta**3
    59	
    60	    N0 = P**2 / Delta**2
    61	    N2 = 2 * P * (P * S2 - Delta * Gw) / Delta**3
    62	    N4 = (Delta**2 * Gw**2 - 2 * Delta * P**2 - 4 * Delta * P * S2 * Gw + 3 * P**2 * S2**2) / Delta**4
    63	
    64	    subs = {
    65	        Q: Q + ell * q1,
    66	        S2: S2 + ell * s1,
    67	        H: H + ell * h1,
    68	        Delta: Delta + ell * d1,
    69	        P: P + ell * p1,
    70	        Gw: Gw + ell * g1,
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    def dlin(expr: sp.Expr) -> sp.Expr:
    74	        return sp.simplify((sp.series(sp.expand(expr.subs(subs)), ell, 0, 2).removeO() - expr) / ell)
    75	
    76	    z0 = dlin(Z0)
    77	    z2 = dlin(Z2)
    78	    z4 = dlin(Z4)
    79	    n0 = dlin(N0)
    80	    n2 = dlin(N2)
    81	    n4 = dlin(N4)
    82	
    83	    assert_zero("z0 closed form", z0 - (Delta * q1 - Q * d1) / Delta**2)
    84	    assert_zero(
    85	        "z2 closed form",
    86	        z2 - (-Delta**2 * h1 + Delta * (H * d1 + Q * s1 + S2 * q1) - 2 * Q * S2 * d1) / Delta**3,
    87	    )
    88	    assert_zero(
    89	        "z4 closed form",
    90	        z4
    91	        - (
    92	            -Delta**2 * H * s1
    93	            - Delta**2 * S2 * h1
    94	            - Delta**2 * q1
    95	            + 2 * Delta * H * S2 * d1
    96	            + 2 * Delta * Q * S2 * s1
    97	            + 2 * Delta * Q * d1
    98	            + Delta * S2**2 * q1
    99	            - 3 * Q * S2**2 * d1
   100	        )
   101	        / Delta**4,
   102	    )
   103	    assert_zero("n0 closed form", n0 - 2 * P * (Delta * p1 - P * d1) / Delta**3)
   104	    assert_zero(
   105	        "n2 closed form",
   106	        n2
   107	        - (
   108	            -(
   109	                2 * Delta**2 * (Gw * p1 + P * g1)
   110	                - 2 * Delta * P * (2 * Gw * d1 + P * s1 + 2 * S2 * p1)
   111	                + 6 * P**2 * S2 * d1
   112	            )
   113	            / Delta**4
   114	        ),
   115	    )
   116	    assert_zero(
   117	        "n4 closed form",
   118	        n4
   119	        - 2
   120	        * (
   121	            Delta**3 * Gw * g1
   122	            - Delta**2 * Gw**2 * d1
   123	            - 2 * Delta**2 * Gw * P * s1
   124	            - 2 * Delta**2 * Gw * S2 * p1
   125	            - 2 * Delta**2 * P * S2 * g1
   126	            - 2 * Delta**2 * P * p1
   127	            + 6 * Delta * Gw * P * S2 * d1
   128	            + 3 * Delta * P**2 * S2 * s1
   129	            + 3 * Delta * P**2 * d1
   130	            + 3 * Delta * P * S2**2 * p1
   131	            - 6 * P**2 * S2**2 * d1
   132	        )
   133	        / Delta**5,
   134	    )
   135	
   136	    slips = {Q: q1, S2: s1, H: h1, Delta: d1, P: p1, Gw: g1}
   137	
   138	    def frechet(expr: sp.Expr) -> sp.Expr:
   139	        return sp.simplify(sum(sp.diff(expr, var) * slip for var, slip in slips.items()))
   140	
   141	    assert_zero("z0 Frechet derivative", z0 - frechet(Z0))
   142	    assert_zero("z2 Frechet derivative", z2 - frechet(Z2))
   143	    assert_zero("z4 Frechet derivative", z4 - frechet(Z4))
   144	    assert_zero("n0 Frechet derivative", n0 - frechet(N0))
   145	    assert_zero("n2 Frechet derivative", n2 - frechet(N2))
   146	    assert_zero("n4 Frechet derivative", n4 - frechet(N4))
   147	
   148	    D0sym, N0sym = sp.symbols("D0 N0", nonzero=True)
   149	    Xi1_static = sp.simplify(n0 / N0 + z0 / D0sym)
   150	    assert_zero(
   151	        "primitive Xi static formula",
   152	        Xi1_static - (2*p1/P - 2*d1/Delta + (Delta*q1 - Q*d1)/(D0sym*Delta**2)),
   153	    )
   154	
   155	    K, B0, Z0slot, N0base, D0target, Ptarget, S, T = sp.symbols("K B0 Z0slot N0base D0target Ptarget S T", nonzero=True)
   156	    K_one_p = sp.solve(
   157	        sp.Eq((K - B0 - (Z0slot + ell * z0)) * (T + ell * z4), 3 * (S + ell * z2) ** 2),
   158	        K,
   159	    )[0]
   160	    K_norm_p = sp.solve(
   161	        sp.Eq((N0base + ell * n0) / (K - B0 - (Z0slot + ell * z0)), Ptarget),
   162	        K,
   163	    )[0]
   164	    compat_direct_p = sp.simplify((N0base + ell * n0) / Ptarget - 3 * (S + ell * z2) ** 2 / (T + ell * z4))
   165	    Ptarget_transport_p = sp.simplify((N0base + ell * n0) / D0target)
   166	    K_norm_transport_p = sp.solve(
   167	        sp.Eq((N0base + ell * n0) / (K - B0 - (Z0slot + ell * z0)), Ptarget_transport_p),
   168	        K,
   169	    )[0]
   170	    compat_transport_p = sp.simplify(K_norm_transport_p - K_one_p)
   171	    compat_direct = compat_direct_p.subs(ell, 0)
   172	    compat_transport = compat_transport_p.subs(ell, 0)
   173	    compat = K_norm_p.subs(ell, 0) - K_one_p.subs(ell, 0)
   174	    dCompat = sp.simplify((sp.series(sp.expand(K_norm_p - K_one_p), ell, 0, 2).removeO() - compat) / ell)
   175	    dCompat_direct = sp.simplify((sp.series(sp.expand(compat_direct_p), ell, 0, 2).removeO() - compat_direct) / ell)
   176	    dCompat_transport = sp.simplify((sp.series(sp.expand(compat_transport_p), ell, 0, 2).removeO() - compat_transport) / ell)
   177	    assert_zero(
   178	        "K_one solve round-trip",
   179	        (K_one_p - B0 - (Z0slot + ell * z0)) * (T + ell * z4) - 3 * (S + ell * z2) ** 2,
   180	    )
   181	    assert_zero(
   182	        "K_norm solve round-trip",
   183	        (N0base + ell * n0) / (K_norm_p - B0 - (Z0slot + ell * z0)) - Ptarget,
   184	    )
   185	    assert_zero(
   186	        "primitive compatibility shift from competing K surfaces",
   187	        dCompat_direct - (n0 / Ptarget - 6 * S * z2 / T + 3 * S**2 * z4 / T**2),
   188	    )
   189	    assert_zero("primitive transported-target normalization K surface", K_norm_transport_p - (B0 + Z0slot + ell * z0 + D0target))
   190	    assert_zero(
   191	        "primitive transported-target compatibility surface",
   192	        compat_transport_p - (D0target - 3 * (S + ell * z2) ** 2 / (T + ell * z4)),
   193	    )
   194	    assert_zero(
   195	        "primitive transported-target compatibility shift",
   196	        dCompat_transport - (-6 * S * z2 / T + 3 * S**2 * z4 / T**2),
   197	    )
   198	    # The derived z0 = (Delta*q1 - Q*d1)/Delta**2 enters K_norm_p and K_one_p
   199	    # through identical +ell*z0 terms, so it must cancel from their difference.
   200	    # The substantive check is that the q1 and d1 partials of the compatibility
   201	    # difference receive NO contribution from the z0 channel: they must equal
   202	    # the q1 and d1 partials of compat_direct_p, which depends only on n0, z2, z4.
   203	    for slip in (q1, d1):
   204	        assert_zero(
   205	            f"primitive fixed-target compatibility has no z0 channel in {slip}",
   206	            sp.diff(K_norm_p - K_one_p, slip) - sp.diff(compat_direct_p, slip),
   207	        )
   208	        assert_zero(
   209	            f"primitive transported-target compatibility has no z0 channel in {slip}",
   210	            sp.diff(K_norm_transport_p - K_one_p, slip) - sp.diff(compat_transport_p, slip),
   211	        )
   212	    # Conversely, the normalization K surface alone DOES retain a q1/d1 channel
   213	    # through the derived z0.
   214	    assert_nonzero(
   215	        "primitive normalization K surface retains q1 channel from z0",
   216	        sp.diff(K_norm_p, q1),
   217	    )
   218	    assert_nonzero(
   219	        "primitive normalization K surface retains d1 channel from z0",
   220	        sp.diff(K_norm_p, d1),
   221	    )
   222	    assert_nonzero(
   223	        "mutated primitive compatibility transport should fail",
   224	        dCompat_direct - (n0 / Ptarget - 6 * S * z2 / T - 3 * S**2 * z4 / T**2),
   225	    )
   226	    assert_nonzero(
   227	        "mutated primitive transported-target compatibility should fail",
   228	        dCompat_transport - (-6 * S * z2 / T - 3 * S**2 * z4 / T**2),
   229	    )
   230	
   231	    lines = []
   232	    lines.append(banner("1) Primitive one-port Maxwell/mixed formulas"))
   233	    lines.append(line("Z0 =", Z0))
   234	    lines.append(line("Z2 =", Z2))
   235	    lines.append(line("Z4 =", Z4))
   236	    lines.append(line("N0 =", N0))
   237	    lines.append(line("N2 =", N2))
   238	    lines.append(line("N4 =", N4))
   239	    lines.append(
   240	        "We now perturb the primitive one-port data by small mouth-local projected-Maxwell slippages:\n"
   241	        "  Q -> Q + ell*q1,\n"
   242	        "  S2 -> S2 + ell*s1,\n"
   243	        "  H  -> H  + ell*h1,\n"
   244	        "  Delta -> Delta + ell*d1,\n"
   245	        "  P -> P + ell*p1,\n"
   246	        "  Gw -> Gw + ell*g1.\n"
   247	    )
   248	
   249	    lines.append(banner("2) Induced bundle corrections z0, z2, z4, n0, n2, n4"))
   250	    lines.append(line("z0 =", z0))
   251	    lines.append(line("z2 =", z2))
   252	    lines.append(line("z4 =", z4))
   253	    lines.append(line("n0 =", n0))
   254	    lines.append(line("n2 =", n2))
   255	    lines.append(line("n4 =", n4))
   256	
   257	    lines.append(banner("3) Reading the primitive slippages"))
   258	    lines.append(
   259	        "Three useful structural facts are immediate:\n"
   260	        "  1. z0 depends only on (q1, d1).\n"
   261	        "  2. z2 depends only on (q1, s1, h1, d1).\n"
   262	        "  3. n0 depends only on (p1, d1).\n"
   263	        "So the first isotropic normalization shift is controlled by the primitive transfer-leg slippage p1,\n"
   264	        "the primitive denominator slippage d1, and the primitive conservative source slippage q1.\n"
   265	    )
   266	
   267	    lines.append(banner("4) Direct primitive contribution to Xi1 = P1/P0"))
   268	    lines.append(line("Xi1^(primitive,static) =", Xi1_static))
   269	    lines.append(
   270	        "So at the one-port primitive level the weak-axisymmetric static prefactor slope receives contributions from:\n"
   271	        "  - transfer-leg slippage p1,\n"
   272	        "  - denominator slippage d1,\n"
   273	        "  - and direct conservative source slippage q1 through z0/D0.\n"
   274	    )
   275	
   276	    lines.append(banner("5) Direct primitive contribution to the isotropic compatibility shift"))
   277	    lines.append(line("delta Compat^(primitive) =", dCompat))
   278	    lines.append(
   279	        "Because delta Compat depends on z2, z4, and n0 only, the primitive contributors that matter here are\n"
   280	        "  q1, s1, h1, d1, p1,\n"
   281	        "while the Gw slope g1 first appears only through n2 and n4, i.e. in the constant-prefactor transport.\n"
   282	        "The script also checks that z0 cancels from both the fixed-target and transported-target compatibility shifts,\n"
   283	        "even though the normalization K surface still carries z0 before compatibility elimination.\n"
   284	    )
   285	
   286	    lines.append(banner("6) Practical reading"))
   287	    lines.append(
   288	        "At this one-port primitive level the projected-Maxwell near-throat correction has a clean hierarchy:\n"
   289	        "  - (q1, d1, p1) are the first static normalization movers,\n"
   290	        "  - (s1, h1) first matter through z2 and z4, hence through one-pole / compatibility transport,\n"
   291	        "  - g1 first matters for N2 and N4, hence for whether the branch stays on the constant-prefactor outgoing surface.\n"
   292	        "\n"
   293	        "This is a useful way to decide which concrete throat-local projected ansatz to try next.\n"
   294	    )
   295	
   296	    print("".join(lines))
   297	    print("STATUS: PASS")
   298	
   299	
   300	if __name__ == "__main__":
   301	    main()

    57	
    58	**Issue:**
    59	The concrete-Gaussian IBP test uses `A_concrete = exp(-w^2)` (even) and `eta_concrete = exp(-w^2/2)` (even). Both the cross integrand `-A * eta * eta_w` and the bulk integrand `A_w * eta^2 / 2` end up odd in `w`, so each integral vanishes by parity over `(-oo, oo)` and the boundary discharge is also 0. The IBP identity asserted at sympy line 69-72 / mathematica line 81 thus reduces to `0 - (0 + 0) = 0`, which is trivially true regardless of whether IBP was applied correctly. The paper-card promise of "both zero and nonzero boundary-discharge probes" is satisfied only by the unrelated `atan(w)` probe on the `boundary_value` operator (sympy line 57-58), not by an asymmetric concrete IBP profile.
    60	
    61	**Required change:**
    62	
    63	In `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage015_parent_throat_action_master_sympy_audit.py`, *after* the existing assertion at line 72 (preserve the Gaussian baseline), insert the following block:
    64	
    65	```python
    66	    # Second concrete probe with asymmetric A profile so the cross and bulk
    67	    # integrals are individually nonzero. Both integrals are even by parity
    68	    # (A_extra is odd, eta is even, products produce even integrands), so
    69	    # the IBP identity asserts a real cancellation, not 0 = 0 + 0.
    70	    A_concrete_asym = w_ibp * sp.exp(-w_ibp**2)
    71	    eta_concrete_asym = sp.exp(-w_ibp**2 / 2)
    72	    quad_boundary_concrete_asym = boundary_value(
    73	        (-A_concrete_asym * eta_concrete_asym**2 / 2), w_ibp
    74	    )
    75	    quad_cross_concrete_asym = sp.integrate(
    76	        -A_concrete_asym * eta_concrete_asym * sp.diff(eta_concrete_asym, w_ibp),
    77	        (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo),
    78	    )
    79	    quad_bulk_concrete_asym = sp.integrate(
    80	        sp.diff(A_concrete_asym, w_ibp) * eta_concrete_asym**2 / 2,
    81	        (w_ibp, -sp.oo, sp.oo),
    82	    )
    83	    assert_nonzero("asymmetric concrete IBP cross is nontrivial", quad_cross_concrete_asym)
    84	    assert_nonzero("asymmetric concrete IBP bulk is nontrivial", quad_bulk_concrete_asym)
    85	    assert_zero("asymmetric concrete quadratic IBP boundary discharge", quad_boundary_concrete_asym)
    86	    assert_zero(
    87	        "asymmetric concrete quadratic IBP with boundary",
    88	        quad_cross_concrete_asym - (quad_boundary_concrete_asym + quad_bulk_concrete_asym),
    89	    )
    90	```

    95	(* Second concrete probe with asymmetric A profile so cross and bulk are
    96	   individually nonzero. Asserts a real IBP cancellation, not 0 = 0 + 0. *)
    97	aConcreteAsym = w*Exp[-w^2];
    98	etaConcreteAsym = Exp[-w^2/2];
    99	boundaryGaussianAsym =
   100	  Quiet[
   101	    Limit[-aConcreteAsym*etaConcreteAsym^2/2, w -> Infinity] -
   102	      Limit[-aConcreteAsym*etaConcreteAsym^2/2, w -> -Infinity],
   103	    Limit::alimv
   104	  ];
   105	crossGaussianAsym =
   106	  Integrate[-aConcreteAsym*etaConcreteAsym*D[etaConcreteAsym, w], {w, -Infinity, Infinity}];
   107	bulkGaussianAsym =
   108	  Integrate[D[aConcreteAsym, w]*etaConcreteAsym^2/2, {w, -Infinity, Infinity}];

   140	
   141	**Issue:**
   142	The K_eta block computes `L2raw = Coefficient[Series[lagrangian, {eps, 0, 2}] // Normal, eps, 2]`, then writes `L2afterIBP = Expand[L2raw - (-TwR0*R0p*eta*etaw) + dTwRR0p*eta^2/2]` and compares it against a pre-written `canonicalL2 = mu0*etat^2/2 - Tw0*etaw^2/2 - TO0*grad2/2 - effectiveMass*eta^2/2`. This mirrors the SymPy script line-by-line (same `L2_raw`/`canonicalL2` decomposition, same `cross_term`/`cross_after_ibp` peeling). A genuine independent derivation should compute K_eta from the Euler-Lagrange operator on the full `lagrangian`, not via series-expansion-and-pattern-match.
   143	
   144	**Required change (K_eta block only):**
   145	
   146	Replace lines 83-102 of `/var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger/mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage015_parent_throat_action_master_mathematica_audit.wl` with the following Euler-Lagrange derivation (the wall-only block at lines 104+ stays untouched until F1 resolution):
   147	
   148	```mathematica
   149	(* M3: K_eta via direct Euler-Lagrange linearization on the parent
   150	   Lagrangian density. We treat R as a field, write R = R0[w] + eps*phi[t,w,Om]
   151	   where phi is the linearized fluctuation, expand the EL equation to O(eps),
   152	   and read off the mass coefficient as -K_eta * phi. This path is independent
   153	   of the SymPy script's series-coefficient choreography. *)
   154	
   155	ClearAll[R0, phi, TwSig, USig, KetaFromEL];
   156	TwSig[r_, w_] := Tw0 + (r - R0[w])*TwR0 + (r - R0[w])^2*TwRR0/2;
   157	USig[r_, w_] := U0 + (r - R0[w])*UR0 + (r - R0[w])^2*URR0/2;
   158	
   159	(* Lagrangian density as function of R(t,w,Om) and its derivatives, with
   160	   |grad_Om R|^2 abbreviated by gO so we do not need an explicit Om mesh. *)
   161	LDensity[R_, Rt_, Rw_, gO_, w_] :=
   162	  mu0*Rt^2/2 - TwSig[R, w]*Rw^2/2 - TO0*gO/2 - USig[R, w];
   163	
   164	(* Euler-Lagrange operator at fixed Om: d_R L - d_t (d_Rt L) - d_w (d_Rw L).
   165	   Compute symbolically with the partials taken as if R, Rt, Rw, gO are
   166	   independent slot variables, then substitute the on-shell expansion. *)
   167	ELRaw[R_, Rt_, Rw_, gO_, w_] :=
   168	  D[LDensity[R, Rt, Rw, gO, w], R] -
   169	    Dt[D[LDensity[R, Rt, Rw, gO, w], Rt], t,
   170	       Constants -> {mu0, Tw0, TO0, U0, TwR0, TwRR0, UR0, URR0}] -
   171	    Dt[D[LDensity[R, Rt, Rw, gO, w], Rw], w,
   172	       Constants -> {mu0, Tw0, TO0, U0, TwR0, TwRR0, UR0, URR0}];
   173	
   174	(* Substitute the linearization R = R0[w] + eps*eta, Rt = eps*etat,
   175	   Rw = R0'[w] + eps*etaw, gO = eps^2*grad2 (transverse term is O(eps^2)). *)
   176	ELLinearized =
   177	  ELRaw[R0[w] + eps*eta, eps*etat, R0'[w] + eps*etaw, eps^2*grad2, w] /.
   178	    {R0'[w] -> R0p,
   179	     Dt[R0p, w, Constants -> {mu0, Tw0, TO0, U0, TwR0, TwRR0, UR0, URR0}] ->
   180	       R0pp,
   181	     Dt[R0[w], w, Constants -> {mu0, Tw0, TO0, U0, TwR0, TwRR0, UR0, URR0}] ->
   182	       R0p};
   183	
   184	(* Take the O(eps) coefficient and read off the eta-coefficient: that is
   185	   -K_eta * eta after the etat-/etaw-derivative terms are stripped. *)
   186	ELOrderEps = Coefficient[Series[ELLinearized, {eps, 0, 1}] // Normal, eps, 1];
   187	ELMassCoeff =
   188	  Coefficient[ELOrderEps /. {Dt[eta, t, ___] -> 0, Dt[eta, w, ___] -> 0,
   189	                              Dt[etat, t, ___] -> 0, Dt[etaw, w, ___] -> 0},
   190	              eta];
   191	(* The EL gives -K_eta * eta + (kinetic / gradient pieces); ELMassCoeff is
   192	   -K_eta. The IBP-derived dTwRR0p stands for d/dw (TwR0(R0,w) * R0'(w)). *)
   193	KetaFromEL =
   194	  -ELMassCoeff /. {Dt[TwR0, w, ___]*R0p + TwR0*R0pp -> dTwRR0p,
   195	                   TwR0*R0pp + Dt[TwR0, w, ___]*R0p -> dTwRR0p};
   196	expectZero["M3 K_eta via EL linearization matches IBP form",
   197	  KetaFromEL - (URR0 - dTwRR0p + TwRR0*R0p^2/2)];
   198	
   199	(* Sign-mutation guard: a + instead of - on dTwRR0p must fail. *)
   200	expectNonzero["M3 K_eta via EL dTwRR0p sign mutation",
   201	  KetaFromEL - (URR0 + dTwRR0p + TwRR0*R0p^2/2)];
   202	```
   203	
   204	After insertion, delete the now-redundant `Tw = Tw0 + eps*TwR0*eta + ...`, `U = ...`, `Rt = eps*etat`, `Rw = R0p + eps*etaw`, `lagrangian = ...`, `L2raw = ...`, `crossCoeff = D[D[L2raw, eta], etaw]`, `expectZero["M3 K_eta raw eta etaw cross coefficient", crossCoeff + TwR0*R0p]`, `effectiveMass = ...`, `canonicalL2 = ...`, `L2afterIBP = ...`, `expectZero["M3 K_eta canonical quadratic form", L2afterIBP - canonicalL2]`, `effectiveMassMutated = ...`, `canonicalL2Mutated = ...`, `expectNonzero["M3 K_eta dTwRR0p sign mutation", ...]` lines (the entire current M3 block, lines 83-102) - their replacement is the EL derivation above.
   205	
   206	If `Dt` substitution proves unreliable (Mathematica `Dt` is finicky about `Constants`), append `## Blocked: F4 (K_eta block)` with the specific Dt error and skip.
   207	
   208	Do NOT modify the wall-only block (lines 104-171) or M8-M9 (lines 173-196); those are blocked on F1.

    76	    lam21 = real_y20_square_ratio(1)

    78	    assert_zero('Y20 overlap lane 20', lam20 - 1)
    79	    assert_zero('Y20 overlap lane 21', lam21 - sp.Rational(1, 2))
    80	    assert_zero('Y20 overlap lane 22', lam22 + 1)
    81	```
    82	
    83	After:
    84	```
    85	    # Anchor the Y20-self overlap to its closed form, so the lane-ratio
    86	    # denominators are not validated against themselves.
    87	    gaunt_base = sp.simplify(gaunt(2, 2, 2, 0, 0, 0))
    88	    assert_zero('Y20 base gaunt closed form', gaunt_base - sp.sqrt(5/sp.pi)/(7*sp.sqrt(sp.pi)/sp.sqrt(1)) * sp.Integer(1))
    89	```
    90	
    91	The exact closed form of `gaunt(2,2,2,0,0,0)` is `sqrt(5/(4*pi)) * 2/7`. Codex: compute this with sympy and replace the closed-form expression in the assertion above with the value sympy actually returns from `sp.sqrt(sp.Rational(5,4)/sp.pi) * sp.Rational(2,7)` — do not invent an answer. If you cannot determine the exact closed form mechanically without running sympy, append `## Blocked: F2` with the question instead.
    92	
    93	In `mathematica_audit.wl`, mirror the same change: introduce
    94	```
    95	gauntBase = FullSimplify[GauntIntegral[2, 2, 2, 0, 0, 0]];
    96	If[!TrueQ[FullSimplify[gauntBase - Sqrt[5/(4 Pi)]*(2/7)] === 0],
    97	  Print["FAIL: M1 gauntBase closed form"]; Exit[1]];
    98	```
    99	before line 35, and leave the existing `lambda0` check in place (it is still tautological, but the new `gauntBase` check now anchors the base value). Codex: insert the new lines but do not delete the existing `lambda0` check; the orchestrator wants both for trace continuity.

# Batch I.2 paper-misalignment resolutions

Six v2 audits surfaced `paper_misalignment` findings. Each is described below with the relevant file:line evidence and (a/b/c/skip) options. Codex: please read the cited paper and script files, then fill the empty `## Recommendation` block under each question with `direction: a|b|c|skip` and a one-paragraph rationale citing file:line. If you do not have a high-confidence answer for a question, write `direction: skip`.

This batch's pattern (like I.1) is dominated by `paper_missing_script_claim`: the EM-projected scripts (stages 013-020) were ported from `notes/em_projected/`, but the paper cards under `paper/stages/` were written later as compact summaries. The natural directions are (a) expand paper card to enumerate the extra script checks; (b) trim scripts to paper scope and migrate extras to whatever stage actually owns them; or (c) add a light paper acknowledgement.

**Files:**
- paper: `paper/stages/stage_013.tex` (whole card; n_0 only; no `deltaP_2/deltaP_4/n_2/n_4` mention)
- sympy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage013_projected_maxwell_mouth_taylor_master_sympy_audit.py:106-151`

- appendix row: `paper/appendices/stage_appendix_part01.tex` row for 013 ("Mouth-local Taylor map for projected source and flux corrections" — too broad to anchor specific deliverables)

**What the script verifies that the paper does not declare:**

2. "Mechanism sieve": 2x2 linear-solve argument that `(K_1, H_even)|_{Sx=Hx=0}` and `(K_1, H_even)|_{Qx=Dx=0}` admit only the trivial pair. No paper-side mention.

- (skip) Codex does not have a high-confidence answer.

**Severity is "high"** because this is roughly half of each script — large fraction of script effort with no paper anchor.

**Options:**

- (a) Expand `paper/stages/stage_015.tex` to declare wall-only gates, Y20 ratios, and grouped trace as additional deliverables of stage 015, and restore (or rewrite) `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage015_*.md` to back them.
- (b) Trim scripts to A1-A8 / M1-M7 (only the K_eta exact-quadratic block, which is the deliverable the paper actually claims); migrate the wall-only/Y20/grouped blocks to whatever stage(s) genuinely own them.
- (skip) Codex does not have a high-confidence answer.

## Recommendation

direction: b
rationale: |
  The wall-only gate obstruction and the real-\(Y_{20}\) lane ratios are already explicit stage 017 outputs (`paper/stages/stage_017.tex:13-37`), while stage 015 only claims the parent action promotion and \(K_\eta\) quadratic recovery (`paper/stages/stage_015.tex:32-46`).  The stage 015 wall-only/Y20/grouped blocks (`scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage015_parent_throat_action_master_sympy_audit.py:103-208`) therefore duplicate later-stage ownership and should be trimmed or migrated rather than promoted into stage 015.

---

## Q4 — Stage 018 (paper_missing_script_claim, medium)

**Files:**
- paper: `paper/stages/stage_018.tex:26-28` (Output paragraph), body 14-22 (only M_Σ, K_Σ branch integrals)
- appendix row 58: "Bundle-level parent-action identities used by the projected electromagnetic response" (plural "identities" hints at more, never enumerated)
- sympy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage018_parent_throat_action_bundle_master_sympy_audit.py:1-2, 25-88`
- mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage018_parent_throat_action_bundle_master_mathematica_audit.wl:1-15, 24-122`
- sympy docstring references nonexistent `step_16_parent_throat_action_bundle_master_notes.md`

**What the script verifies that the paper does not declare:**

Paper Output says only M_Σ, K_Σ branch integrals. Scripts additionally verify:

1. An algebraic identity about coefficients of a quartic pole expansion (one-pole numerator).
2. Two `KSigma` closure conditions and their compatibility (one-pole vs. normalization closures).
3. A 2x2 even-gate determinant equal to `1/27` and closed-form wall-stiffness/wall-inertia slopes.
4. A residual `Xi1` after applying those slopes.

Only the trailing Gaussian integrals A18/A19 / M8 directly correspond to the paper's M_Σ, K_Σ deliverables, and they do so only for a specific concrete profile (`μ_η = T_w = K_η + 6 T_Ω = 1`, `β = exp(-w²/2)`).

**Options:**

- (a) Expand the paper card and restore `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage018_*.md` to declare the four extra claim families (one-pole closure, gate determinant, wall slopes, Xi_1 residual) as 018 deliverables.
- (b) Move the four extra claim families to a different stage (candidates: 015-017 or 021 — "Reduced Maxwell/mixed one-port normal form"); script becomes just the bridge identity check.
- (c) Prune scripts to just the M_Σ, K_Σ bridge identity (drop families 1-4 entirely).
- (skip) Codex does not have a high-confidence answer.

## Recommendation

direction: b
rationale: |
  Stage 018 should own the parent-wall bridge integrals \(M_\Sigma,K_\Sigma\) (`paper/stages/stage_018.tex:12-28`), while the one-pole/normalization compatibility is already stage 019 content (`paper/stages/stage_019.tex:13-46`) and the even-gate slope solve plus residual \(\Xi_1\) is stage 020 content (`paper/stages/stage_020.tex:38-55`).  The extra stage 018 script families (`scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage018_parent_throat_action_bundle_master_sympy_audit.py:25-88`) should move to those owning stages or be removed from stage 018 once confirmed there.

---

## Q5 — Stage 020 (paper_missing_script_claim, medium)

**Files:**
- paper: `paper/stages/stage_020.tex:1-56` (no Y20 / Gaunt / lane ratio mention anywhere in card)
- appendix row: `paper/appendices/stage_appendix_part01.tex:62` ("Weak-axisymmetric packet exported from the parent throat-action bundle" — no Y20 mention)
- sympy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage020_parent_throat_action_weak_axisym_packet_sympy_audit.py:45-50`
- mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage020_parent_throat_action_weak_axisym_packet_mathematica_audit.wl:34-67`

**What the script verifies that the paper does not declare:**

Both engines compute and assert Y20-squared lane overlap ratios `λ_{20} = 1, λ_{21} = 1/2, λ_{22} = -1` and vanishing of same-sign m-cross terms. The paper card and appendix row make no reference to spherical harmonics, Y20 overlaps, Gaunt integrals, or these lane ratios. Paper card content is purely the 1D wall-slope solve in the `β_2(w)` profile and identities `eq:stage020-wall-slopes` through `eq:stage020-residual-xi`.

The "weak-axisymmetric" content of the stage title suggests the Y20 ratios are conceptually load-bearing somewhere — but the paper does not say where.

**Options:**

- (a) Amend `paper/stages/stage_020.tex` to add a paragraph or equation citing the Y20 lane ratios as part of the weak-axisymmetric deliverable.
- (b) Remove the Y20 block from both scripts (sympy:45-50, wl:34-67) — they belong to a different stage.
- (skip) Codex does not have a high-confidence answer.

## Recommendation

direction: b
rationale: |
  The \(Y_{20}\) ratios are not used by the stage 020 wall-slope solve after they are asserted (`scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage020_parent_throat_action_weak_axisym_packet_sympy_audit.py:45-70`), and stage 020's paper deliverable is the algebraic weak-axisymmetric packet from \(\delta M_\Sigma,\delta K_\Sigma\) through the compensated \(\Xi_1\) (`paper/stages/stage_020.tex:12-55`).  The angular lane law is already paper-owned upstream by stage 017 (`paper/stages/stage_017.tex:13-37`) and elsewhere by the broader grouped-bundle/overlap stages, so the duplicate Y20 block should be removed from stage 020.

---

## Q6 — Stage 021 (script_missing_paper_claim, medium)

**This is the inverse direction**: paper claims more than scripts assert.

**Files:**
- paper: `paper/stages/stage_021.tex:77-81` (Output paragraph)
- paper: `paper/stages/stage_021.tex:71-75` (`eq:app-stage021-wall-odd`)
- sympy: `scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage021_reduced_one_port_normal_form_sympy_audit.py:238-244` (`Dcorr` computed and printed, no assertion)
- mathematica: `mathematica/moving_throat_pde_stage021_reduced_one_port_normal_form_mathematica_audit.wl:135, 140` (`dCorr` computed and printed, no assertion)

**What the paper claims that the script does not verify:**

The paper's Output paragraph explicitly enumerates three exports: (1) reduced one-port self-energy, (2) transfer factor, (3) **wall-level outgoing quadrupole coefficient** `δD_2^(odd)(ω) = -i N_2(0) (a^5/(27 c_s^5)) ω^5 + O(ω^7)`. The numeric coefficient `a^5/(27 c_s^5)` is substituted in for `Γ_5^port`.

In both scripts, Section III computes:
```
Dcorr = -I * Gamma_port * omega^5 * N0   (sympy)
dCorr = -I * gammaPort * omega^5 * n0    (mathematica)
```
`Gamma_port` is a free symbolic constant; it is never specialized to `a^5/(27 c_s^5)` and `Dcorr` is never asserted against the paper's closed form. Sections III and IV separately verify N_l(0) and Γ_5^port; the composition is asserted only in prose, not in code.

The fix is algebraically trivial (one substitution + assertion in each script).

**Options:**

- (a) Add the composed assertion to both scripts (sympy and mathematica) — paper is right, scripts have a verification gap. **Recommended:** the auditor flagged paper-says-three-the-scripts-prove-two-and-a-half as the natural read.
- (b) Trim the paper Output to two exports (drop the wall-level odd-coefficient claim) — the composition is trivial enough that the paper can describe it in prose without claiming it as a deliverable.
- (skip) Codex does not have a high-confidence answer.

## Recommendation

direction: a
rationale: |
  The paper and the stage note both make the composed wall-level odd quadrupole coefficient a headline export (`paper/stages/stage_021.tex:60-81`, `notes/stages/moving_throat_pde_stage021_reduced_one_port_normal_form.md:229-244`).  The scripts separately verify \(N_l(0)\) and \(\Gamma_5^{\rm port}=a^5/(27c_s^5)\), but only print the unspecialized `Dcorr`/`dCorr` (`scripts/moving_throat_pde_stage021_reduced_one_port_normal_form_sympy_audit.py:227-244`), so adding the composed assertion is the correct low-risk fix.

---
